task-0239-edit-distance · Edit distance

Prompt 
Write `min_distance(word1, word2)` in Python returning the minimum number of operations (insert, delete, replace) to convert word1 into word2.

Output ONLY the function definition `def min_distance(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def min_distance(word1, word2):
    m, n = len(word1), len(word2)
    dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
    for i in range(m + 1):
        dp[i][0] = i
    for j in range(n + 1):
        dp[0][j] = j
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if word1[i - 1] == word2[j - 1]:
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1]
            else:
                dp[i][j] = 1 + min(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1], dp[i - 1][j - 1])
    return dp[m][n]

task-0181-product-of-array-except-self · Product of array except self

Prompt 
Write `productExceptSelf(nums)` returning an array such that answer[i] is the product of all elements of nums except nums[i]. Must run in O(n) without using division.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `productExceptSelf`.
Model answer 
export function productExceptSelf(nums) {
    const n = nums.length;
    const res = new Array(n).fill(1);
    let prefix = 1;
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        res[i] = prefix;
        prefix *= nums[i];
    }
    let suffix = 1;
    for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        res[i] *= suffix;
        suffix *= nums[i];
    }
    return res;
}

task-0202-find-the-difference · Find the difference

Prompt 
Write `findTheDifference(s, t)` where `t` is formed by shuffling `s` and adding one extra lowercase letter. Return the added letter.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findTheDifference`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function findTheDifference(s, t) {
  let res = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) res ^= s.charCodeAt(i);
  for (let i = 0; i < t.length; i++) res ^= t.charCodeAt(i);
  return String.fromCharCode(res);
}

task-0076-secretary-task-ordering · Secretary task ordering

Prompt 
A scheduling secretary receives a list of `tasks`, where each task is [priority, labelIndex] but here each task is [priority, label]. Write `secretary(tasks)` returning the array of labels ordered by ascending priority; when two tasks share a priority, keep their original (input) order (a stable sort). The label is a string, priority is a non-negative integer.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function secretary(tasks) {
  return tasks
    .map((task, index) => ({ priority: task[0], label: task[1], index }))
    .sort((a, b) => a.priority - b.priority || a.index - b.index)
    .map(item => item.label);
}

task-0139-maximum-product-subarray · Maximum product subarray

Prompt 
Write `maxProduct(nums)` returning the maximum product of any contiguous (non-empty) subarray. `nums` can contain negatives and zeros. O(n) with tracking max/min.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxProduct(nums) {
  let maxSoFar = nums[0];
  let minSoFar = nums[0];
  let result = nums[0];
  for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const num = nums[i];
    if (num < 0) {
      [maxSoFar, minSoFar] = [minSoFar, maxSoFar];
    }
    maxSoFar = Math.max(num, maxSoFar * num);
    minSoFar = Math.min(num, minSoFar * num);
    result = Math.max(result, maxSoFar);
  }
  return result;
}

task-0102-zero-downtime-service-migration · Zero-downtime service migration

Prompt 
You must migrate a running service from an old cluster to a new one with zero user-visible downtime. Describe the ordered plan: start new instances, verify their health checks pass, drain connections from the old instances, shift traffic at the load balancer, and confirm you have a rollback plan in case the new instances fail.
Model answer 
To guarantee zero user-visible downtime, the technically correct execution order is:

1. **Start New Instances:** Deploy the service to the new cluster and register them with the load balancer in a `standby` or `unhealthy` state to prevent premature routing.
2. **Verify Health Checks:** Run automated health probes (HTTP/TCP/custom). Confirm consistent passes across all new instances before marking them `healthy` in the load balancer.
3. **Shift Traffic at Load Balancer:** Gradually increase traffic weight to the new cluster (e.g., 10% → 50% → 100%) while monitoring error rates, latency, and throughput. This overlapping phase is essential for zero downtime.
4. **Drain Connections from Old Instances:** Once 100% traffic is routed to the new cluster, enable connection draining on the old instances. Wait for in-flight requests to complete and new connections to cease, then deregister them. *(Note: Draining must follow traffic shift; draining before would cut off users.)*
5. **Confirm Rollback Plan:** Pre-configure the load balancer to instantly revert weights to 100% old cluster. Keep old instances running until the new cluster demonstrates stability over a defined observation window. If new instances fail, immediately revert traffic and investigate.

task-0217-next-permutation · Next permutation

Prompt 
Write `nextPermutation(nums)` returning the next lexicographic permutation of the array (as a new array). If it's already the greatest permutation, wrap around to the smallest (ascending). Example: `nextPermutation([1,2,3])` returns `[1,3,2]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `nextPermutation`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function nextPermutation(nums) {
    const arr = [...nums];
    const n = arr.length;
    if (n <= 1) return arr;

    let i = n - 2;
    while (i >= 0 && arr[i] >= arr[i + 1]) {
        i--;
    }

    if (i >= 0) {
        let j = n - 1;
        while (arr[j] <= arr[i]) {
            j--;
        }
        [arr[i], arr[j]] = [arr[j], arr[i]];
    }

    let left = i + 1;
    let right = n - 1;
    while (left < right) {
        [arr[left], arr[right]] = [arr[right], arr[left]];
        left++;
        right--;
    }

    return arr;
}

task-0287-reverse-integer · Reverse integer

Prompt 
Reverse the digits of an integer, keeping its sign, dropping any leading zeros in the result. Example: 123 -> 321, -120 -> -21. All results fit in 32-bit int range.

```js
// BUGGY
export function reverseInt(x) {
  const rev = Number(String(x).split("").reverse().join(""));
  return rev;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `reverseInt`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function reverseInt(x) {
  const sign = x < 0 ? -1 : 1;
  const absX = x < 0 ? -x : x;
  const rev = Number(String(absX).split("").reverse().join(""));
  return sign * rev;
}

task-0215-rotate-array-right-by-k · Rotate array right by k

Prompt 
Write `rotate(nums, k)` rotating the array to the right by `k` steps (modular: k may exceed the length). Return the rotated array. Example: `rotate([1,2,3,4,5,6,7], 3)` returns `[5,6,7,1,2,3,4]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `rotate`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function rotate(nums, k) {
  if (nums.length === 0) return nums;
  k %= nums.length;
  return nums.slice(-k).concat(nums.slice(0, -k));
}

task-0086-lru-cache-operator · LRU cache operator

Prompt 
A cache agent manages an LRU (least-recently-used) cache of `capacity` slots. It processes a list of key `accesses` (integers). A 'hit' occurs when the key is already in the cache (it is moved to most-recent); a 'miss' occurs otherwise. On a miss the key is inserted, and if the cache is full the least-recently-used key is evicted. Write `lruAccess(capacity, accesses)` returning the total number of cache misses. If capacity is 0, every access is a miss.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function lruAccess(capacity, accesses) {
  if (capacity <= 0) return accesses.length;
  const cache = new Map();
  let misses = 0;
  for (const key of accesses) {
    if (cache.has(key)) {
      cache.delete(key);
      cache.set(key, true);
    } else {
      misses++;
      if (cache.size === capacity) {
        cache.delete(cache.keys().next().value);
      }
      cache.set(key, true);
    }
  }
  return misses;
}

task-0123-merge-intervals · Merge intervals

Prompt 
Write `merge(intervals)` merging all overlapping intervals. `intervals` is an array of `[start, end]` pairs (end inclusive). Return the merged intervals sorted by start.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function merge(intervals) {
  if (!intervals || intervals.length === 0) return [];
  
  const sorted = [...intervals].sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
  const merged = [sorted[0].slice()];
  
  for (let i = 1; i < sorted.length; i++) {
    const last = merged[merged.length - 1];
    const current = sorted[i];
    
    if (current[0] <= last[1]) {
      last[1] = Math.max(last[1], current[1]);
    } else {
      merged.push(current.slice());
    }
  }
  
  return merged;
}

task-0283-roman-to-integer · Roman to integer

Prompt 
Convert a valid Roman numeral string to an integer, handling subtractive forms (IV=4, IX=9, XL=40, etc.).

```js
// BUGGY
export function romanToInt(s) {
  const v = { I: 1, V: 5, X: 10, L: 50, C: 100, D: 500, M: 1000 };
  let total = 0;
  for (const ch of s) total += v[ch];
  return total;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `romanToInt`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function romanToInt(s) {
  const v = { I: 1, V: 5, X: 10, L: 50, C: 100, D: 500, M: 1000 };
  let total = 0;
  let prev = 0;
  for (let i = s.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const curr = v[s[i]];
    if (curr < prev) {
      total -= curr;
    } else {
      total += curr;
    }
    prev = curr;
  }
  return total;
}

task-0229-largest-number-formed-by-joining · Largest number formed by joining

Prompt 
Write `largestNumber(nums)` arranging the numbers to form the largest possible value when their string forms are concatenated, returned as a string. If the result would start with zeros (all zeros), return "0".

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `largestNumber`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function largestNumber(nums) {
  const strNums = nums.map(String);
  strNums.sort((a, b) => (b + a) > (a + b) ? 1 : -1);
  const result = strNums.join('');
  return result[0] === '0' ? '0' : result;
}

task-0045-cap-theorem-precise-statement · CAP Theorem Precise Statement

Prompt 
State the CAP theorem precisely. Name the three guarantees, and explain which guarantee a CP system sacrifices when a network partition occurs.
Model answer 
**The CAP Theorem** states that in a distributed data store, it is impossible to simultaneously guarantee more than two out of the following three properties during any given network partition:

1.  **Consistency (C):** Every read receives the most recent write or an error. (All nodes see the same data at the same time).
2.  **Availability (A):** Every request receives a (non-error) response, without the guarantee that it contains the most recent write.
3.  **Partition Tolerance (P):** The system continues to operate despite an arbitrary number of messages being dropped or delayed by the network between nodes.

**The CP Sacrifice**
When a network partition occurs, a CP (Consistency/Partition Tolerance) system sacrifices **Availability**. 

Because the network is split, nodes in different partitions cannot communicate to synchronize their data. To maintain strict consistency and prevent nodes from serving stale, outdated, or conflicting data, the system must halt operations on the isolated partition(s). It will refuse to accept writes (and often reads) until the network heals and the nodes can successfully synchronize.
Reasoning Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0137-wiggle-subsequence-length · Wiggle subsequence length

Prompt 
Write `wiggleMaxLength(nums)` returning the length of the longest subsequence that alternates strictly up and down (wiggle). Consecutive equal values collapse. Example `[1,7,4,9,2,5]` -> 6.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function wiggleMaxLength(nums) {
  if (nums.length === 0) return 0;
  let up = 1;
  let down = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i] > nums[i - 1]) {
      up = down + 1;
    } else if (nums[i] < nums[i - 1]) {
      down = up + 1;
    }
  }
  return Math.max(up, down);
}

task-0074-elevator-commute-distance · Elevator commute distance

Prompt 
An elevator agent starts at floor 0 and services a list of floor `requests` one at a time, travelling directly between consecutive floors. Write `elevator(requests)` returning the total number of floors travelled to service all requests in the given order. Each request is a non-negative integer floor.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function elevator(requests) {
  let total = 0;
  let current = 0;
  for (const r of requests) {
    total += Math.abs(r - current);
    current = r;
  }
  return total;
}

task-0233-max-consecutive-ones-with-one-flip · Max consecutive ones with one flip

Prompt 
Write `find_max_consecutive_ones(nums)` in Python. The binary array contains 0s and 1s. You may flip at most one 0 to 1. Return the length of the longest subarray of 1s achievable.

Output ONLY the function definition `def find_max_consecutive_ones(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def find_max_consecutive_ones(nums):
    left = 0
    max_len = 0
    zero_count = 0
    for right in range(len(nums)):
        if nums[right] == 0:
            zero_count += 1
        while zero_count > 1:
            if nums[left] == 0:
                zero_count -= 1
            left += 1
        max_len = max(max_len, right - left + 1)
    return max_len

task-0146-minimum-path-in-grid · Minimum path in grid

Prompt 
Write `minPathSum(grid)` returning the minimum sum from top-left to bottom-right moving only down or right. `grid` is m x n of non-negative numbers. DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function minPathSum(grid) {
    const m = grid.length;
    const n = grid[0].length;
    for (let i = 0; i < m; i++) {
        for (let j = 0; j < n; j++) {
            if (i === 0 && j === 0) continue;
            if (i === 0) {
                grid[i][j] += grid[i][j - 1];
            } else if (j === 0) {
                grid[i][j] += grid[i - 1][j];
            } else {
                grid[i][j] += Math.min(grid[i - 1][j], grid[i][j - 1]);
            }
        }
    }
    return grid[m - 1][n - 1];
}

task-0031-modular-exponentiation · Modular exponentiation

Prompt 
Write `mod_pow(base, exp, m)` in Python returning (base^exp) mod m. Use fast modular exponentiation (repeated squaring); do not compute base^exp directly. `m` is positive and exp >= 0. Return the non-negative integer remainder.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def mod_pow(base, exp, m):
    res = 1 % m
    base %= m
    while exp > 0:
        if exp % 2 == 1:
            res = (res * base) % m
        exp //= 2
        base = (base * base) % m
    return res

task-0027-exactly-two-heads-in-three-flips · Exactly two heads in three flips

Prompt 
A fair coin is flipped 3 times. What is the probability of getting exactly 2 heads?
Model answer 
The user wants to find the probability of getting exactly 2 heads when a fair coin is flipped 3 times.

1.  **Identify the total number of outcomes:**
    Each flip has 2 possible outcomes (Heads or Tails).
    Since there are 3 flips, the total number of outcomes is $2^3 = 8$.
    The sample space is: {HHH, HHT, HTH, HTT, THH, THT, TTH, TTT}.

2.  **Identify the favorable outcomes:**
    We want exactly 2 heads.
    Looking at the sample space:
    - HHH (3 heads) - No
    - HHT (2 heads) - Yes
    - HTH (2 heads) - Yes
    - HTT (1 head) - No
    - THH (2 heads) - Yes
    - THT (1 head) - No
    - TTH (1 head) - No
    - TTT (0 heads) - No

    The favorable outcomes are {HHT, HTH, THH}.
    There are 3 favorable outcomes.

    Alternatively, using combinations:
    We need to choose 2 positions for Heads out of 3 flips.
    $\binom{3}{2} = \frac{3!}{2!(3-2)!} = \frac{3 \times 2 \times 1}{2 \times 1 \times 1} = 3$.

3.  **Calculate the probability:**
    $P(\text{exactly 2 heads}) = \frac{\text{Number of favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total number of outcomes}}$
    $P = \frac{3}{8}$

4.  **Format the answer:**
    The user requested the single best option, preferably just the letter or the option text. Since there are no multiple-choice options provided in the prompt, I should provide the calculated value. However, usually, these benchmarks imply a multiple-choice context or just want the direct answer.
    Wait, the prompt says "Answer the question with the single best option. Prefer replying with just the letter (e.g. "B") optionally followed by the option text."
    But there are no options A, B, C, D provided.
    In this case, I should provide the answer directly.
    The answer is 3/8 or 0.375.

    Let me double-check if I missed options.
    "A fair coin is flipped 3 times. What is the probability of getting exactly 2 heads?"
    No options listed.
    I will provide the fraction and the decimal.
    Actually, looking at the instruction "Prefer replying with just the letter...", this suggests a multiple-choice format was intended but missing.
    However, I must answer the question asked.
    The answer is 3/8.

    Let's assume the standard format for such questions if options were present:
    A) 1/8
    B) 3/8
    C) 1/2
    D) 5/8
    The answer would be B.

    Since I cannot guess the letters, I will provide the value.
    Value: 3/8.

    Let's re-read carefully. "Answer the question with the single best option."
    If no options are given, the "option" is the answer itself.
    I will write "3/8".

    Wait, is there a hidden trick?
    Fair coin, 3 flips.
    Outcomes: HHH, HHT, HTH, HTT, THH, THT, TTH, TTT. (8 total)
    Exactly 2 heads: HHT, HTH, THH. (3 total)
    Probability: 3/8.

    I will output "3/8".

    Let's check if the prompt implies a specific format like "C) 3/8". No.
    I'll just provide the answer.

    Wait, maybe the user *is* the benchmark and expects me to act as if I'm selecting from a list that isn't there? No, that's overthinking.
    I will provide the mathematical answer.

    Let's consider if the user made a mistake and forgot the options.
    If I have to choose a letter, I can't.
    I will provide the text "3/8".

    Let's try to interpret "single best option" as the answer itself.
    Answer: 3/8.

    Let's verify the calculation again.
    Binomial distribution $B(n, p)$.
    $n = 3$, $p = 0.5$.
    $P(X=k) = \binom{n}{k} p^k (1-p)^{n-k}$.
    $k = 2$.
    $P(X=2) = \binom{3}{2} (0.5)^2 (0.5)^1 = 3 \times 0.25 \times 0.5 = 3 \times 0.125 = 0.375$.

task-0176-palindrome-number · Palindrome number

Prompt 
Write `is_palindrome(x)` in Python that returns True if the integer `x` reads the same forward and backward, WITHOUT converting to string. Handle negatives (not palindromes).

Output ONLY the function `is_palindrome`.
Model answer 
def is_palindrome(x):
    if x < 0:
        return False
    original = x
    rev = 0
    while x > 0:
        rev = rev * 10 + x % 10
        x //= 10
    return rev == original
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0142-burst-balloons-max-coins · Burst balloons max coins

Prompt 
Write `max_coins(nums)` in Python. Balloons numbered 0..n-1 have value in `nums`. Popping balloon i earns `nums[i-1] * nums[i] * nums[i+1]` (out-of-bound balloons are treated as value 1). Return the maximum coins obtainable by popping all balloons. Use DP over intervals.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def max_coins(nums):
    n = len(nums)
    A = [1] + nums + [1]
    dp = [[0] * (n + 2) for _ in range(n + 2)]
    
    for length in range(2, n + 2):
        for i in range(n + 2 - length):
            j = i + length
            for k in range(i + 1, j):
                dp[i][j] = max(dp[i][j], dp[i][k] + dp[k][j] + A[i] * A[k] * A[j])
                
    return dp[0][n + 1]
Reasoning Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0293-happy-number · Happy number

Prompt 
Return true if n is a happy number: repeatedly replace n with the sum of the squares of its digits, until it becomes 1 (true) or cycles forever (false). Example: 19 -> true, 2 -> false.

```js
// BUGGY
export function isHappy(n) {
  const seen = new Set();
  while (n !== 1) {
    let s = 0;
    while (n > 0) {
      const d = n % 10;
      s += d * d;
      n = Math.floor(n / 10);
    }
    n = s;
  }
  return true;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `isHappy`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function isHappy(n) {
  const seen = new Set();
  while (n !== 1) {
    if (seen.has(n)) return false;
    seen.add(n);
    let s = 0;
    while (n > 0) {
      const d = n % 10;
      s += d * d;
      n = Math.floor(n / 10);
    }
    n = s;
  }
  return true;
}

task-0171-two-sum-return-indices · Two Sum — return indices

Prompt 
Write a function `twoSum(nums, target)` that returns the indices of the two numbers that add up to `target`. You may assume exactly one solution exists, and you may not use the same element twice. Return the indices in any order.

Output ONLY the function definition (an ES module exporting `twoSum`), no explanation, no wrapper script.
Model answer 
export function twoSum(nums, target) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const complement = target - nums[i];
    if (map.has(complement)) {
      return [map.get(complement), i];
    }
    map.set(nums[i], i);
  }
}

task-0075-stack-operator · Stack operator

Prompt 
A data-structure agent runs a stack. It processes a list of `cmds`, each either 'push <value>' or 'pop'. When a 'pop' is processed and the stack is non-empty, the popped value is recorded; if the stack is empty the pop is ignored. Write `stackOps(cmds)` returning the array of popped values in the order they were popped. Values are arbitrary strings without spaces.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function stackOps(cmds) {
  const stack = [];
  const result = [];
  for (const cmd of cmds) {
    if (cmd.startsWith('push ')) {
      stack.push(cmd.slice(5));
    } else if (cmd === 'pop') {
      if (stack.length) {
        result.push(stack.pop());
      }
    }
  }
  return result;
}
Code Comprehension Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0006-bitwise-and-of-powers · Bitwise AND of powers

Prompt 
```js
console.log(12 & 10);
```
What number does this print?
Model answer 
It prints **8**.

**Explanation:**
The `&` operator performs a **bitwise AND** operation on the 32-bit binary representations of the numbers:
* `12` in binary is `0000...001100`
* `10` in binary is `0000...001010`
* Bitwise AND (`1100 & 1010`) yields `1000`, which is **8** in decimal.

task-0081-turnstile-event-simulator · Turnstile event simulator

Prompt 
A metro agent simulates a turnstile. It begins in the LOCKED state. Each event is either 'coin' or 'pass'. A 'coin' unlocks the turnstile (if already unlocked it stays unlocked). A 'pass' while unlocked lets one person through (counts the pass) and locks the turnstile; a 'pass' while locked is ignored (and the turnstile stays locked). Write `turnstile(events)` applying the events in order and returning an array [finalState, passes] where finalState is 'locked' or 'unlocked' and passes is the total number of successful passes.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function turnstile(events) {
  let state = 'locked';
  let passes = 0;
  for (const event of events) {
    if (event === 'coin') {
      state = 'unlocked';
    } else if (event === 'pass' && state === 'unlocked') {
      passes++;
      state = 'locked';
    }
  }
  return [state, passes];
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0225-word-pattern · Word pattern

Prompt 
Write `wordPattern(pattern, s)` returning true if `s` follows the same pattern, where each letter in `pattern` maps to exactly one word in `s` (bijection, one-to-one both ways). Example: `wordPattern("abba", "dog cat cat dog")` is true.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `wordPattern`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function wordPattern(pattern, s) {
    const words = s.split(' ');
    if (words.length !== pattern.length) return false;
    const map1 = new Map();
    const map2 = new Map();
    for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
        if (map1.has(pattern[i]) && map1.get(pattern[i]) !== words[i]) return false;
        if (map2.has(words[i]) && map2.get(words[i]) !== pattern[i]) return false;
        map1.set(pattern[i], words[i]);
        map2.set(words[i], pattern[i]);
    }
    return true;
}

100%

task-0285-move-zeroes · Move zeroes

Prompt 
Move all zeros to the end while keeping the relative order of non-zero elements. Return the array. Example: [0,1,0,3,12] -> [1,3,12,0,0].

```js
// BUGGY
export function moveZeroes(nums) {
  let i = 0;
  for (const n of nums) {
    if (n !== 0) nums[i++] = n;
  }
  return nums;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `moveZeroes`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function moveZeroes(nums) {
  let i = 0;
  for (let j = 0; j < nums.length; j++) {
    if (nums[j] !== 0) {
      nums[i++] = nums[j];
    }
  }
  while (i < nums.length) {
    nums[i++] = 0;
  }
  return nums;
}

task-0304-largest-number-from-array · Largest number from array

Prompt 
Arrange the numbers so they form the largest possible number when concatenated. Return the result as a string (no leading zeros unless the number is 0). Example: [3,30,34,5,9] -> "9534330".

```python
# BUGGY
import functools
def largest_number(nums):
    def cmp(a, b):
        return -1 if a + b < b + a else 1
    s = [str(n) for n in nums]
    s.sort(key=functools.cmp_to_key(cmp))
    res = "".join(s)
    return res
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def largest_number(nums):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
import functools
def largest_number(nums):
    def cmp(a, b):
        if a + b > b + a:
            return -1
        elif a + b < b + a:
            return 1
        return 0
    s = [str(n) for n in nums]
    s.sort(key=functools.cmp_to_key(cmp))
    res = "".join(s)
    return "0" if res[0] == '0' else res

task-0196-group-anagrams · Group anagrams

Prompt 
Write `groupAnagrams(strs)` grouping strings that are anagrams together. Within each group and across groups, order does not matter for correctness but output should be an array of groups (each group an array of strings).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `groupAnagrams`.
Model answer 
export function groupAnagrams(strs) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (const s of strs) {
    const key = s.split('').sort().join('');
    if (!map.has(key)) map.set(key, []);
    map.get(key).push(s);
  }
  return Array.from(map.values());
}

task-0238-lru-cache-operation-sequence · LRU cache (operation sequence)

Prompt 
Write `lruCache(capacity, operations)` implementing an LRU (least recently used) cache. Each operation is either ["get", key] or ["put", key, value]. get returns the value or -1 if absent (and marks it most recently used). put inserts/updates (marking most recently used), evicting the least recently used key when over capacity. Return an array of the results in order, using null for put operations.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `lruCache`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function lruCache(capacity, operations) {
  const cache = new Map();
  const results = [];

  for (const op of operations) {
    if (op[0] === "get") {
      const key = op[1];
      if (cache.has(key)) {
        const val = cache.get(key);
        cache.delete(key);
        cache.set(key, val);
        results.push(val);
      } else {
        results.push(-1);
      }
    } else if (op[0] === "put") {
      const key = op[1];
      const value = op[2];
      if (cache.has(key)) {
        cache.delete(key);
      }
      if (cache.size === capacity) {
        const firstKey = cache.keys().next().value;
        cache.delete(firstKey);
      }
      cache.set(key, value);
      results.push(null);
    }
  }

  return results;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0133-daily-temperatures · Daily temperatures

Prompt 
Write `dailyTemperatures(temperatures)` returning an array where answer[i] is the number of days until a warmer temperature (strictly greater), or 0 if no warmer day comes. Monotonic stack, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function dailyTemperatures(temperatures) {
    const n = temperatures.length;
    const result = new Array(n).fill(0);
    const stack = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        while (stack.length > 0 && temperatures[stack[stack.length - 1]] < temperatures[i]) {
            const prevIndex = stack.pop();
            result[prevIndex] = i - prevIndex;
        }
        stack.push(i);
    }
    return result;
}

task-0240-burst-balloons · Burst balloons

Prompt 
Write `maxCoins(nums)`. You burst balloons to collect coins; bursting balloon i yields nums[i-1] * nums[i] * nums[i+1] (treat out-of-range as 1). Find the maximum coins you can collect by choosing the burst order.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `maxCoins`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function maxCoins(nums) {
    const n = nums.length;
    const A = [1, ...nums, 1];
    const dp = Array.from({ length: n + 2 }, () => Array(n + 2).fill(0));
    
    for (let len = 2; len <= n + 1; len++) {
        for (let i = 0; i <= n + 1 - len; i++) {
            const j = i + len;
            let maxVal = 0;
            for (let k = i + 1; k < j; k++) {
                const val = dp[i][k] + dp[k][j] + A[i] * A[k] * A[j];
                if (val > maxVal) maxVal = val;
            }
            dp[i][j] = maxVal;
        }
    }
    
    return dp[0][n + 1];
}

```js
export function compress(s) {

  let out = "";
  let c = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < s.length; i++) {
    if (s[i] === s[i - 1]) {
      c++;

      out += s[i - 1] + c;
      c = 1;
```

task-0242-basic-calculator-ii · Basic calculator II

Prompt 
Write `calculate(s)` evaluating a string expression containing non-negative integers and the operators +, -, *, / (integer division truncating). Respect operator precedence. The expression is valid. Example: `calculate("3+2*2")` returns 7.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `calculate`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function calculate(s) {
  let pos = 0;
  const str = s.replace(/\s/g, '');
  
  function parseExpr() {
    let val = parseTerm();
    while (pos < str.length && (str[pos] === '+' || str[pos] === '-')) {
      const op = str[pos++];
      const right = parseTerm();
      val = op === '+' ? val + right : val - right;
    }
    return val;
  }
  
  function parseTerm() {
    let val = parseFactor();
    while (pos < str.length && (str[pos] === '*' || str[pos] === '/')) {
      const op = str[pos++];
      const right = parseFactor();
      val = op === '*' ? val * right : Math.trunc(val / right);
    }
    return val;
  }
  
  function parseFactor() {
    let num = 0;
    while (pos < str.length && str[pos] >= '0' && str[pos] <= '9') {
      num = num * 10 + +str[pos];
      pos++;
    }
    return num;
  }
  
  return parseExpr();
}

    max_profit = 0
    for price in prices:
        if price < min_price:
            min_price = price

task-0152-count-palindromic-substrings · Count palindromic substrings

Prompt 
Write `countSubstrings(s)` returning the number of palindromic substrings in `s` (a single character counts as a palindrome). Expand around centers, O(n^2).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function countSubstrings(s) {
  let count = 0;
  const n = s.length;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    let l = i, r = i;
    while (l >= 0 && r < n && s[l] === s[r]) {
      count++;
      l--;
      r++;
    }
    l = i;
    r = i + 1;
    while (l >= 0 && r < n && s[l] === s[r]) {
      count++;
      l--;
      r++;
    }
  }
  return count;
}

task-0083-bracket-validator · Bracket validator

Prompt 
A syntax-checking agent validates a string containing only the characters '(', ')', '{', '}', '[', ']'. Write `balanced(s)` returning true if the brackets are correctly nested and every opener has a matching closer, else false. Example: '([{}])' -> true, '([)]' -> false.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function balanced(s) {
  const stack = [];
  const map = { ')': '(', '}': '{', ']': '[' };
  for (const c of s) {
    if (c === '(' || c === '{' || c === '[') {
      stack.push(c);
    } else if (c === ')' || c === '}' || c === ']') {
      if (stack.pop() !== map[c]) return false;
    }
  }
  return stack.length === 0;
}

task-0132-subarray-sum-equals-k-count · Subarray sum equals k count

Prompt 
Write `subarraySum(nums, k)` returning the number of contiguous subarrays whose sum equals `k`. Use a prefix-sum hash map for O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function subarraySum(nums, k) {
    const prefixSumCount = new Map();
    prefixSumCount.set(0, 1);
    let currentSum = 0;
    let count = 0;
    for (const num of nums) {
        currentSum += num;
        if (prefixSumCount.has(currentSum - k)) {
            count += prefixSumCount.get(currentSum - k);
        }
        prefixSumCount.set(currentSum, (prefixSumCount.get(currentSum) || 0) + 1);
    }
    return count;
}

task-0236-median-of-two-sorted-arrays · Median of two sorted arrays

Prompt 
Write `findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2)` returning the median of the two sorted arrays combined. If the combined length is even, return the average of the two middle numbers (e.g. 2.5). O(log(m+n)).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findMedianSortedArrays`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2) {
    if (nums1.length > nums2.length) {
        [nums1, nums2] = [nums2, nums1];
    }
    
    let m = nums1.length;
    let n = nums2.length;
    let left = 0;
    let right = m;
    
    while (left <= right) {
        let i = Math.floor((left + right) / 2);
        let j = Math.floor((m + n + 1) / 2) - i;
        
        let nums1LeftMax = (i === 0) ? -Infinity : nums1[i - 1];
        let nums1RightMin = (i === m) ? Infinity : nums1[i];
        let nums2LeftMax = (j === 0) ? -Infinity : nums2[j - 1];
        let nums2RightMin = (j === n) ? Infinity : nums2[j];
        
        if (nums1LeftMax <= nums2RightMin && nums2LeftMax <= nums1RightMin) {
            if ((m + n) % 2 === 1) {
                return Math.max(nums1LeftMax, nums2LeftMax);
            } else {
                return (Math.max(nums1LeftMax, nums2LeftMax) + Math.min(nums1RightMin, nums2RightMin)) / 2;
            }
        } else if (nums1LeftMax > nums2RightMin) {
            right = i - 1;
        } else {
            left = i + 1;
        }
    }
    
    return 0.0;
}
Reasoning Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0121-hamming-weight · Hamming weight

Prompt 
Write `hammingWeight(n)` returning the number of 1 bits in the 32-bit unsigned binary representation of non-negative integer `n`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function hammingWeight(n) {
    n >>>= 0;
    let count = 0;
    while (n > 0) {
        n &= n - 1;
        count++;
    }
    return count;
}

task-0109-design-a-self-healing-autoscaling-strate · Design a self-healing autoscaling strategy

Prompt 
Design a resilient autoscaling strategy for an API that must absorb traffic spikes automatically. Describe the ordered design: collect the right metrics (CPU, latency, queue depth), define autoscaling thresholds, run health checks that remove unhealthy instances, automatically scale instance count up and down, and include alerting plus a manual override in case automation misbehaves.
Model answer 
Here is a resilient, ordered autoscaling strategy for an API:

**1. Collect the Right Metrics**
- **Metrics:** CPU utilization, request latency (p50/p95/p99), and queue depth (in-flight requests or message backlog).
- **Collection:** Instrument the API with OpenTelemetry/statsd. Forward to a time-series DB (Prometheus, CloudWatch, Datadog).
- **Aggregation:** Compute rolling 1–5 minute windows. Aggregate per-instance values, then fleet-wide averages/percentiles. Exclude cold-start outliers by ignoring metrics from instances <2 min old.

**2. Define Autoscaling Thresholds**
- **Scale-Up Triggers:** CPU > 70%, p95 latency > 500ms, or queue depth > 100 requests.
- **Scale-Down Triggers:** CPU < 30%, p95 latency < 200ms, and queue depth < 10 requests.
- **Policy:** Use a "most restrictive" rule for scaling up (any breach triggers) and an "all-clear" rule for scaling down (all metrics must be low). Enforce cooldowns (3–5 min after scale-up, 5–10 min after scale-down) to prevent flapping. Set hard bounds (e.g., min=2, max=50).

**3. Run Health Checks & Remove Unhealthy Instances**
- **Probes:** Lightweight HTTP/TCP checks against `/health` (liveness) and `/ready` (readiness). Require <200ms response, 200 status, and successful dependency pings (DB/cache).
- **Failure Handling:** 3 consecutive failures mark an instance unhealthy. The load balancer stops routing traffic. After a 2–3 minute grace period, the autoscaler terminates the instance and provisions a replacement.
- **Resilience:** Use separate readiness/liveness probes. Ensure health checks are idempotent and don't trigger cascading failures.

**4. Automatically Scale Instance Count Up and Down**
- **Scale-Up:** Add instances in batches (e.g., +20% or +2, min 1). Pre-warm new instances (warm connection pools, load caches) before marking them ready.
- **Scale-Down:** Remove instances in reverse order. Gracefully drain active connections (grace period + active request completion). Only scale down when cooldowns and all thresholds are met.
- **Execution:** Use target-tracking or step-scaling policies backed by cloud-native controllers (AWS ASG, GCP MIG, K8s HPA). Log every scaling event with timestamp, reason, and delta.

**5. Alerting & Manual Override**
- **Alerting:** Monitor autoscaler health, scaling success/failure rates, and metric anomalies. Alert on: scaling failures, persistent threshold breaches >5 min, health check failure rate >10%, or override engagement. Route to PagerDuty/Slack with runbook links.
- **Manual Override:** Provide a UI/CLI toggle to disable autoscaling, set a fixed instance count, or pause scaling. Require audit logging and operator confirmation. When engaged, the system immediately reverts to manual mode, halts automated adjustments, and surfaces a clear "MANUAL MODE" status.
- **Recovery:** Override remains active until manually disengaged. Include a one-click "resume autoscaling" that validates current metrics before re-enabling policies.

This design ensures rapid spike absorption, prevents flapping and cascading failures, maintains service health, and guarantees operator control when automation deviates.
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases

task-0209-find-nearest-valid-point · Find nearest valid point

Prompt 
Write `nearestValidPoint(x, y, points)` where each point is [xi, yi]. A point is valid if it shares the same x or y coordinate as (x, y). Return the index of the valid point with the smallest Manhattan distance |x-xi| + |y-yi|; on a tie return the smallest index. Return -1 if no valid point.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `nearestValidPoint`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function nearestValidPoint(x, y, points) {
  let minDist = Infinity;
  let resultIndex = -1;
  for (let i = 0; i < points.length; i++) {
    const xi = points[i][0];
    const yi = points[i][1];
    if (xi === x || yi === y) {
      const dist = Math.abs(x - xi) + Math.abs(y - yi);
      if (dist < minDist) {
        minDist = dist;
        resultIndex = i;
      }
    }
  }
  return resultIndex;
}

task-0130-max-water-container · Max water container

Prompt 
Write `maxArea(height)` where `height[i]` is the height of a vertical line at position i. Return the maximum area of water that two lines and the x-axis (distance between their indices) can hold. O(n) two pointers.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxArea(height) {
  let left = 0;
  let right = height.length - 1;
  let maxArea = 0;
  while (left < right) {
    const currentArea = Math.min(height[left], height[right]) * (right - left);
    maxArea = Math.max(maxArea, currentArea);
    if (height[left] < height[right]) {
      left++;
    } else {
      right--;
    }
  }
  return maxArea;
}

task-0301-sliding-window-maximum · Sliding window maximum

Prompt 
Return an array of the max of each contiguous window of size k. Example: nums=[1,3,-1,-3,5,3,6,7], k=3 -> [3,3,5,5,6,7].

```python
# BUGGY
def max_sliding_window(nums, k):
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    out = []
    for i, n in enumerate(nums):
        while q and nums[q[-1]] <= n:
            q.pop()
        q.append(i)
        if q[0] <= i - k:
            q.popleft()
        if i >= k:
            out.append(nums[q[0]])
    return out
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def max_sliding_window(nums, k):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def max_sliding_window(nums, k):
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    out = []
    for i, n in enumerate(nums):
        while q and nums[q[-1]] <= n:
            q.pop()
        q.append(i)
        if q[0] <= i - k:
            q.popleft()
        if i >= k - 1:
            out.append(nums[q[0]])
    return out
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0079-grid-pathfinding-agent · Grid pathfinding agent

Prompt 
A delivery agent must find the shortest path across a grid. `grid` is an m x n matrix of 0 (open) and 1 (obstacle). `start` and `goal` are [row, col]. The agent moves up/down/left/right one cell at a time and cannot enter obstacles or leave the grid. Write `shortestSteps(grid, start, goal)` returning the minimum number of moves to reach the goal, or -1 if it is unreachable. (If already at the goal, return 0.)

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function shortestSteps(grid, start, goal) {
    const m = grid.length;
    const n = grid[0].length;
    const [sr, sc] = start;
    const [gr, gc] = goal;

    if (sr === gr && sc === gc) return 0;

    const visited = Array.from({ length: m }, () => Array(n).fill(false));
    visited[sr][sc] = true;

    const queue = [[sr, sc, 0]];
    let head = 0;
    const directions = [[-1, 0], [1, 0], [0, -1], [0, 1]];

    while (head < queue.length) {
        const [r, c, steps] = queue[head++];

        for (const [dr, dc] of directions) {
            const nr = r + dr;
            const nc = c + dc;

            if (nr >= 0 && nr < m && nc >= 0 && nc < n && 
                grid[nr][nc] === 0 && !visited[nr][nc]) {
                
                if (nr === gr && nc === gc) {
                    return steps + 1;
                }
                
                visited[nr][nc] = true;
                queue.push([nr, nc, steps + 1]);
            }
        }
    }

    return -1;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 13/13 cases
100%

task-0197-string-to-integer-atoi · String to integer (atoi)

Prompt 
Write `myAtoi(s)` implementing the atoi algorithm: ignore leading whitespace; optional +/- sign; read digits; clamp to 32-bit signed range [-2^31, 2^31-1]. If no digits, return 0.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `myAtoi`.
Model answer 
export function myAtoi(s) {
    let i = 0;
    const n = s.length;
    
    while (i < n && s[i] === ' ') i++;
    if (i === n) return 0;
    
    let sign = 1;
    if (s[i] === '+') {
        i++;
    } else if (s[i] === '-') {
        sign = -1;
        i++;
    }
    
    let result = 0;
    const INT_MAX = 2147483647;
    const INT_MIN = -2147483648;
    
    while (i < n && s[i] >= '0' && s[i] <= '9') {
        result = result * 10 + (s[i] - '0');
        if (result > INT_MAX) {
            return sign === 1 ? INT_MAX : INT_MIN;
        }
        i++;
    }
    
    return sign * result;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 5/5 cases
100%

task-0185-running-sum · Running sum

Prompt 
Write `runningSum(nums)` returning an array where each element is the cumulative sum of nums up to that index.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `runningSum`.
Model answer 
export function runningSum(nums) {
  const result = [];
  let sum = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    sum += nums[i];
    result[i] = sum;
  }
  return result;
}

task-0159-longest-mountain · Longest mountain

Prompt 
Write `longest_mountain(arr)` in Python returning the length of the longest subarray that is a mountain: strictly increasing then strictly decreasing, length >= 3. Return 0 if none.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def longest_mountain(arr):
    n = len(arr)
    if n < 3:
        return 0
    
    left = [0] * n
    right = [0] * n
    
    for i in range(1, n):
        if arr[i-1] < arr[i]:
            left[i] = left[i-1] + 1
            
    for i in range(n-2, -1, -1):
        if arr[i] > arr[i+1]:
            right[i] = right[i+1] + 1
            
    max_len = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if left[i] > 0 and right[i] > 0:
            max_len = max(max_len, left[i] + right[i] + 1)
            
    return max_len

task-0140-longest-increasing-subsequence · Longest increasing subsequence

Prompt 
Write `lis_length(nums)` in Python returning the length of the longest strictly increasing subsequence. O(n log n) using patience sorting.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
import bisect

def lis_length(nums):
    tails = []
    for x in nums:
        i = bisect.bisect_left(tails, x)
        if i < len(tails):
            tails[i] = x
        else:
            tails.append(x)
    return len(tails)
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases

task-0226-isomorphic-strings · Isomorphic strings

Prompt 
Write `is_isomorphic(s, t)` in Python returning True if the characters in `s` can be replaced to get `t`, as a one-to-one mapping of characters (no two characters map to the same character).

Output ONLY the function definition `def is_isomorphic(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
The user wants a Python function `is_isomorphic(s, t)` that checks if two strings are isomorphic.
Isomorphic strings mean that there's a one-to-one mapping between characters of `s` and characters of `t`.
This means:
1. Every character in `s` maps to exactly one character in `t`.
2. Every character in `t` maps to exactly one character in `s`.
3. The mapping must be consistent throughout the strings.
4. The strings must be of the same length.

Let's implement this efficiently.
We can use two dictionaries (or arrays since characters are ASCII/Unicode, but dicts are fine) to keep track of the mappings from `s` to `t` and `t` to `s`.
Iterate through both strings simultaneously.
For each pair `(c1, c2)`:
- If `c1` is in `map_s_to_t` and `map_s_to_t[c1] != c2`, return False.
- If `c2` is in `map_t_to_s` and `map_t_to_s[c2] != c1`, return False.
- Otherwise, add the mappings: `map_s_to_t[c1] = c2`, `map_t_to_s[c2] = c1`.
If the loop completes, return True.

Edge cases:
- Different lengths: return False immediately.
- Empty strings: return True (vacuously true, or loop won't execute).

Let's write it out:
```python
def is_isomorphic(s, t):
    if len(s) != len(t):
        return False
    map_s_to_t = {}
    map_t_to_s = {}
    for c1, c2 in zip(s, t):
        if c1 in map_s_to_t:
            if map_s_to_t[c1] != c2:
                return False
        else:
            map_s_to_t[c1] = c2
            
        if c2 in map_t_to_s:
            if map_t_to_s[c2] != c1:
                return False
        else:
            map_t_to_s[c2] = c1
    return True
```

Wait, we can simplify:
```python
def is_isomorphic(s, t):
    if len(s) != len(t):
        return False
    m1, m2 = {}, {}
    for c1, c2 in zip(s, t):
        if m1.get(c1) != m2.get(c2):
            return False
        m1[c1] = c2
        m2[c2] = c1
    return True
```
This is a known trick for isomorphic strings. Let's verify:
Initially both empty.
First pair: `m1.get(c1)` is None, `m2.get(c2)` is None. They match. Set `m1[c1]=c2`, `m2[c2]=c1`.
Second pair: if `c1` seen before, `m1.get(c1)` is the previous `c2`. If `c2` matches previous `c2`, `m2.get(c2)` will be the same previous `c1`. So they match.
If `c1` seen before but maps to different `c2`, `m1.get(c1)` != `c2`, but `m2.get(c2)` might be None or some other `c1`. So `m1.get(c1) != m2.get(c2)` will be True, returns False. Correct.
If `c2` seen before but maps to different `c1`, similar logic. Correct.
This trick works perfectly and is concise.

Let's double check with an example:
s = "egg", t = "add"
1. e, a: m1.get('e')=None, m2.get('a')=None. Match. m1={'e':'a'}, m2={'a':'e'}
2. g, d: m1.get('g')=None, m2.get('d')=None. Match. m1={'e':'a', 'g':'d'}, m2={'a':'e', 'd':'g'}
3. g, d: m1.get('g')='d', m2.get('d')='g'. Match.
Returns True. Correct.

s = "foo", t = "bar"
1. f, b: match. m1={'f':'b'}, m2={'b':'f'}
2. o, a: match. m1={'f':'b', 'o':'a'}, m2={'b':'f', 'a':'o'}
3. o, r: m1.get('o')='a', m2.get('r')=None. 'a' != None -> False. Correct.

s = "ab", t = "aa"
1. a, a: match. m1={'a':'a'}, m2={'a':'a'}
2. b, a: m1.get('b')=None, m2.get('a')='a'. None != 'a' -> False. Correct.

The trick works.
One minor point: `m1.get(c1) != m2.get(c2)` compares values. If both are None, it's False (they are equal), so it continues. If one is None and other is not, it's True, returns False. If both are not None, compares the mapped characters. This is correct.

Wait, what if `m1.get(c1)` is `None` and `m2.get(c2)` is `None`? `None != None` is False, so it continues. Correct.
What if `m1.get(c1)` is `'x'` and `m2.get(c2)` is `'x'`? `'x' != 'x'` is False, continues. Correct.
What if `m1.get(c1)` is `'x'` and `m2.get(c2)` is `'y'`? `'x' != 'y'` is True, returns False. Correct.

So the condition `if m1.get(c1) != m2.get(c2): return False` is perfectly correct.

Let's stick to this concise version.
Is there any case where `m1.get(c1)` and `m2.get(c2)` could be equal but shouldn't be?
Suppose `c1` maps to `c2`, and `c2` maps to `c1`. They are stored as `m1[c1]=c2` and `m2[c2]=c1`.
When we encounter a new pair `(c3, c4)`, we check `m1.get(c3)` vs `m2.get(c4)`.
If `c3` was never seen, `m1.get(c3)` is None. If `c4` was never seen, `m2.get(c4)` is None. They match.
If `c3` was seen, `m1.get(c3)` is some `v1`. If `c4` was seen, `m2.get(c4)` is some `v2`. We check `v1 != v2`.
This correctly enforces that the mapping is consistent.

One thing to consider: characters could be anything, but `get` returns `None` by default. That's fine.

Let's write out the function exactly as requested.
```python

def is_isomorphic(s, t):
    if len(s) != len(t):
        return False
    m1, m2 = {}, {}
    for c1, c2 in zip(s, t):
        if c1 in m1 and m1[c1] != c2:
            return False
        if c2 in m2 and m2[c2] != c1:
            return False
        m1[c1] = c2
        m2[c2] = c1
    return True
```

Is there any simpler way?
Using `set` and `zip`:
```python
def is_isomorphic(s, t):
    return len(set(zip(s, t))) == len(set(s)) == len(set(t))
```
This is a known one-liner for isomorphic strings. Let's verify it.
`zip(s, t)` creates pairs. `set(zip(s, t))` gets unique pairs.
`set(s)` gets unique chars in s.
`set(t)` gets unique chars in t.
If isomorphic, each char in s maps to exactly one char in t, and vice versa. So number of unique pairs equals number of unique chars in s, which equals number of unique chars in t.
Example: s="egg", t="add"
pairs: ('e','a'), ('g','d'), ('g','d') -> set has 2 pairs.
set(s): {'e', 'g'} -> 2
set(t): {'a', 'd'} -> 2
2 == 2 == 2 -> True. Correct.

Example: s="foo", t="bar"
pairs: ('f','b'), ('o','a'), ('o','r') -> set has 3 pairs.
set(s): {'f', 'o'} -> 2
3 == 2 -> False. Correct.

Example: s="ab", t="aa"
pairs: ('a','a'), ('b','a') -> set has 2 pairs.
set(s): {'a', 'b'} -> 2
set(t): {'a'} -> 1
2 == 2 == 1 -> False. Correct.

This one-liner is elegant and correct. But is it efficient? It creates sets, which is O(N) time and space. It's fine.
However, the explicit dict approach is more standard and often preferred in interviews for clarity and early exit. I'll stick to the explicit dict approach as it's robust and clearly demonstrates the mapping logic. The prompt doesn't restrict to one-liners.

I will provide the explicit dict version. It's safe and standard.

task-0305-number-of-islands · Number of islands

Prompt 
Given an m x n 2D grid of '1's (land) and '0's (water), return the number of islands. An island is a group of adjacent lands connected horizontally/vertically. Example: a 4x5 grid with two '1' clusters -> 2.

```python
# BUGGY
def num_islands(grid):
    if not grid:
        return 0
    rows, cols = len(grid), len(grid[0])
    count = 0
    def dfs(r, c):
        if r < 0 or r >= rows or c < 0 or c >= cols or grid[r][c] == "0":
            return
        grid[r][c] = "0"
        dfs(r + 1, c)
        dfs(r - 1, c)
        dfs(r, c + 1)
    for r in range(rows):
        for c in range(cols):
            if grid[r][c] == "1":
                count += 1
                dfs(r, c)
    return count
```

def num_islands(grid):
    if not grid:
        return 0

Let's think about digit DP.
We want to count numbers in `[1, n]` that do not contain digit `d`.
This is equivalent to counting numbers in `[0, n]` that do not contain `d`, then subtract 1 if `0` is counted (since range is 1..n). Actually, 0 doesn't contain any digit except '0', so if `d == 0`, 0 contains `d`. If `d != 0`, 0 does not contain `d`. But we want range 1..n.
Let's just count numbers in `[0, n]` that don't contain `d`, then adjust.
Actually, it's easier to count numbers in `[1, n]` directly.

Digit DP state: `(index, tight, started)`
`index`: current digit position (from left to right, 0 to len-1)
`tight`: boolean, whether we are restricted by the digits of `n`
`started`: boolean, whether we have placed a non-zero digit yet (to handle leading zeros)

Wait, leading zeros don't count as digits. So if `started` is False, we can place 0 and remain `started=False`. If we place a non-zero digit, `started` becomes True.
Once `started` is True, we cannot place digit `d`.

Let's define `s = str(n)`
`dp(index, tight, started)` returns count of valid suffixes.

Base case: `index == len(s)` -> return 1 (we formed a valid number, including 0 if started=False)
But we want to count numbers in 1..n. So we can count 0..n and subtract 1 if 0 is counted.
Actually, if `started` is False at the end, it means we formed the number 0. So we can just return 1 for that case, and later subtract 1.

Transitions:
Limit for current digit: `int(s[index])` if `tight` else 9.
Iterate `digit` from 0 to limit:
  new_tight = tight and (digit == limit)
  if not started and digit == 0:
    new_started = False
  else:
    new_started = True
  if new_started and digit == d:

Let's write it out carefully.

```python
from functools import lru_cache

def count_no_digit(n, d):
    if n < 1:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    m = len(s)
    
    @lru_cache(None)
    def dp(idx, tight, started):
        if idx == m:
            return 1
        limit = int(s[idx]) if tight else 9
        res = 0
        for digit in range(limit + 1):
            new_tight = tight and (digit == limit)
            if not started and digit == 0:
                new_started = False
            else:
                new_started = True
            if new_started and digit == d:
                continue
            res += dp(idx + 1, new_tight, new_started)
        return res

    return dp(0, True, False) - 1
```

```python
from functools import lru_cache

def count_no_digit(n, d):
    if n < 1:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    m = len(s)
    
    @lru_cache(None)
    def dp(idx, tight, started):
        if idx == m:
            return 1
        limit = int(s[idx]) if tight else 9
        res = 0
        for digit in range(limit + 1):
            new_tight = tight and (digit == limit)
            if not started and digit == 0:
                new_started = False
            else:
                new_started = True
            if new_started and digit == d:
                continue
            res += dp(idx + 1, new_tight, new_started)
        return res

    return dp(0, True, False) - 1
```

task-0113-count-primes-up-to-n · Count primes up to n

Prompt 
Write `countPrimes(n)` returning the number of prime numbers strictly less than `n`. Use a sieve for efficiency. `n` is in [0, 10^6].

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function countPrimes(n) {
  if (n <= 2) return 0;
  const isPrime = new Array(n).fill(true);
  isPrime[0] = false;
  isPrime[1] = false;
  for (let p = 2; p * p < n; p++) {
    if (isPrime[p]) {
      for (let i = p * p; i < n; i += p) {
        isPrime[i] = false;
      }
    }
  }
  let count = 0;
  for (let i = 2; i < n; i++) {
    if (isPrime[i]) count++;
  }
  return count;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0144-trapping-rain-water · Trapping rain water

Prompt 
Write `trap(height)` in Python returning how much water can be trapped between the bars described by `height`. O(n) two-pointer or left/right max arrays.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def trap(height):
    left, right = 0, len(height) - 1
    left_max, right_max = 0, 0
    water = 0
    while left < right:
        if height[left] < height[right]:
            if height[left] >= left_max:
                left_max = height[left]
            else:
                water += left_max - height[left]
            left += 1
        else:
            if height[right] >= right_max:
                right_max = height[right]
            else:
                water += right_max - height[right]
            right -= 1
    return water
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0180-container-with-most-water · Container with most water

Prompt 
Write `maxArea(height)` where height[i] is the height of a vertical line at i. Return the maximum area of water a container can hold (two lines + x-axis). Use two pointers, O(n).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `maxArea`.
Model answer 
export function maxArea(height) {
  let left = 0;
  let right = height.length - 1;
  let maxArea = 0;
  while (left < right) {
    maxArea = Math.max(maxArea, (right - left) * Math.min(height[left], height[right]));
    if (height[left] < height[right]) {
      left++;
    } else {
      right--;
    }
  }
  return maxArea;
}

task-0107-downtime-free-database-schema-migration · Downtime-free database schema migration

Prompt 
You must add columns and restructure a core table in a large live database without downtime or data loss. Describe the ordered migration plan: create the new schema in parallel, backfill data into the new columns, switch the application to dual-write/read from both old and new, then cut over reads, and detail how you would verify and roll back.
Model answer 
Here is the ordered migration plan, structured for zero downtime and zero data loss:

**1. Parallel Schema Creation**
- **Action:** Execute online DDL to add new columns or create a parallel table structure.
- **Implementation:** Use non-blocking DDL (`ALGORITHM=INPLACE` in MySQL, `CONCURRENTLY` in PostgreSQL, or equivalent). Deploy application code with schema versioning/feature flags to handle both old and new structures.
- **Safety:** Online DDL avoids table locks; feature flags enable instant code revert without schema changes.

**2. Backfill Data**
- **Action:** Populate new columns from existing data.
- **Implementation:** Run an idempotent, batched backfill job (e.g., 1k–10k rows per transaction). Use a checkpoint table to track progress and resume on failure. Process during low-traffic windows or with throttling to avoid replication lag and lock contention.
- **Safety:** Idempotency prevents duplicate writes; batching limits impact on query latency and replication.

**3. Switch to Dual-Write/Read**
- **Action:** Enable application logic to write to and read from both schemas.
- **Implementation:** 
  - *Writes:* Update old and new columns within a single transaction (or use DB triggers if supported) to guarantee atomicity.
  - *Reads:* Initially route reads to the old schema; new writes populate the new columns in real-time.
  - Enable a feature flag to toggle dual-write on/off.
- **Safety:** Dual-write ensures no data loss from concurrent writes; old schema remains the authoritative read source during transition.

**4. Read Cutover**
- **Action:** Shift read traffic to the new schema.
- **Implementation:** Once backfill is 100% complete and consistency checks pass, use a routing layer or feature flag to gradually shift reads (canary → 10% → 50% → 100%). Monitor latency, error rates, and replication lag at each step.
- **Safety:** Traffic shifting isolates failures; old columns remain untouched, preserving instant rollback capability.

**5. Verification & Rollback Strategy**
- **Verification:**
  - *Data:* Automated reconciliation (row counts, hash/checksum comparisons, random spot checks) between old and new columns.
  - *Application:* A/B testing of read paths, performance baselining, and error-rate monitoring against pre-migration SLOs.
  - *Operational:* Alerting on replication lag, lock waits, and dual-write transaction failures.
- **Rollback:**
  - *During backfill/dual-write:* Disable dual-write flag. Revert app to old schema only. Backfill can be paused/resumed safely.
  - *During read cutover:* Flip read flag back to old schema. Since old columns were never dropped, zero data loss occurs.
  - *Post-cutover:* If structural or performance issues emerge, revert app code and routing. Final cleanup (dropping old columns) is deferred until a multi-week stability window.
- **Safety:** All state changes are reversible via flags; no destructive DDL occurs until post-stability. Transactional dual-write and idempotent backfill guarantee data integrity throughout.
Mathematics Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0033-dice-sum-ways · Dice sum ways

Prompt 
Write `dice_ways(k, s)` in Python returning the number of ordered outcomes of rolling `k` standard six-sided dice (each showing 1 through 6) whose values sum exactly to `s`. Return 0 if the sum is impossible. Use DP over the number of dice.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def dice_ways(k, s):
    if s < k or s > 6 * k:
        return 0
    dp = [0] * (s + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for _ in range(k):
        new_dp = [0] * (s + 1)
        for j in range(1, s + 1):
            for face in range(1, 7):
                if j >= face:
                    new_dp[j] += dp[j - face]
        dp = new_dp
    return dp[s]
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0224-house-robber · House robber

Prompt 
Write `rob(nums)` returning the maximum amount you can steal from non-adjacent houses, where nums[i] is the money in house i (you cannot rob two adjacent houses).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `rob`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function rob(nums) {
  let prev2 = 0, prev1 = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    const current = Math.max(prev1, prev2 + num);
    prev2 = prev1;
    prev1 = current;
  }
  return prev1;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 10/10 cases

task-0179-roman-to-integer · Roman to Integer

Prompt 
Write `roman_to_int(s)` in Python converting a valid Roman numeral string to an integer.

Output ONLY the function `roman_to_int`.
Model answer 
def roman_to_int(s):
    roman_map = {'I': 1, 'V': 5, 'X': 10, 'L': 50, 'C': 100, 'D': 500, 'M': 1000}
    total = 0
    for i in range(len(s)):
        value = roman_map[s[i]]
        if i < len(s) - 1 and value < roman_map[s[i+1]]:
            total -= value
        else:
            total += value
    return total

task-0294-product-of-array-except-self · Product of array except self

Prompt 
Return an array where answer[i] is the product of all elements except nums[i], in O(n) without division. Example: [1,2,3,4] -> [24,12,8,6].

```js
// BUGGY
export function productExceptSelf(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const out = new Array(n).fill(1);
  let left = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    out[i] = left;
    left *= nums[i];
  }
  let right = 1;
  for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    right *= nums[i];
    out[i] *= right;
  }
  return out;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `productExceptSelf`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function productExceptSelf(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const out = new Array(n).fill(1);
  let left = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    out[i] = left;
    left *= nums[i];
  }
  let right = 1;
  for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    out[i] *= right;
    right *= nums[i];
  }
  return out;
}

task-0093-chemical-formula-counter · Chemical formula counter

Prompt 
A chemistry agent counts atoms in a chemical formula. `formula` is a valid string where an element is an uppercase letter followed by zero or more lowercase letters (e.g. 'H', 'O', 'Mg'), an element or parenthesized group may be followed by a positive integer count, and parentheses may nest. Write `count_atoms(formula)` returning a dict mapping each element (string) to its total count, with keys sorted alphabetically. Example: count_atoms('Mg(OH)2') -> {'H': 2, 'Mg': 1, 'O': 2}.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def f(...)`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
The user wants a function `count_atoms(formula)` that parses a chemical formula and returns a dictionary of element counts.
The formula can contain:
- Elements: uppercase letter followed by zero or more lowercase letters.
- Parentheses: can be nested.
- Counts: positive integers following an element or a parenthesized group.

Example: `Mg(OH)2` -> `{'H': 2, 'Mg': 1, 'O': 2}`

I need to write a parser. Since parentheses can be nested, a stack-based approach or recursive descent parser is suitable.
Let's use a stack-based approach.
We can iterate through the string and maintain a stack of dictionaries representing the current scope.
When we encounter an opening parenthesis `(`, we push a new dictionary onto the stack.
When we encounter a closing parenthesis `)`, we pop the top dictionary, multiply its counts by the following number (if any), and merge it into the new top dictionary.
When we encounter an element, we parse its name and count, and add it to the current top dictionary.

Let's refine the stack approach:
Initialize `stack = [{}]`
Current index `i = 0`
While `i < len(formula)`:
  char = formula[i]
  if char == '(':
    stack.append({})
    i += 1
  elif char == ')':
    i += 1
    # parse number
    start = i
    while i < len(formula) and formula[i].isdigit():
      i += 1
    multiplier = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
    # pop and merge
    top = stack.pop()
    for elem, count in top.items():
      stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count * multiplier
  elif char.isupper():
    # parse element name
    start = i
    i += 1
    while i < len(formula) and formula[i].islower():
      i += 1
    elem = formula[start:i]
    # parse count
    start = i
    while i < len(formula) and formula[i].isdigit():
      i += 1
    count = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
    # add to current scope
    stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count
  else:
    # should not happen for valid formulas, but skip or handle error
    i += 1

After processing, `stack[0]` contains the final counts.
Return `dict(sorted(stack[0].items()))`

Let's test this logic mentally with `Mg(OH)2`:
stack = [{}]
i=0, char='M' -> upper
  parse elem: "Mg"
  parse count: none -> 1
  stack[-1]['Mg'] = 1
  stack = [{'Mg': 1}]
i=2, char='(' -> push new dict
  stack = [{'Mg': 1}, {}]

  elem: "O", count: 1
  stack[-1]['O'] = 1
  stack = [{'Mg': 1}, {'O': 1}]

  elem: "H", count: 1
  stack[-1]['H'] = 1
  stack = [{'Mg': 1}, {'O': 1, 'H': 1}]
i=5, char=')' -> pop and merge
  parse count: "2" -> multiplier = 2
  top = {'O': 1, 'H': 1}
  stack[-1] (which is [{'Mg': 1}]) gets updated:
    'O': 1*2 = 2
    'H': 1*2 = 2
  stack = [{'Mg': 1, 'O': 2, 'H': 2}]
End of string.
Return sorted dict: {'H': 2, 'Mg': 1, 'O': 2}. Matches example.

Another example: `K4(ON(SO3)2)2`

  merge into stack[1]: {'O':1, 'N':2, 'S':4, 'O':12} -> {'O':13, 'N':2, 'S':4}
) -> wait, the formula is `K4(ON(SO3)2)2`. After `)2`, we are at end.

Edge cases:
- Single element: `H` -> `{'H': 1}`
- Element with count: `H2` -> `{'H': 2}`
- Nested parentheses: `(H2O)3` -> `{'H': 6, 'O': 3}`
- Multiple elements: `H2O` -> `{'H': 2, 'O': 1}`

- Counts can be multi-digit: `H12` -> `{'H': 12}`

The parsing logic seems robust.
One detail: `while i < len(formula) and formula[i].isdigit():`
This correctly handles multi-digit numbers.
What about `formula[i].islower()`? Correct.

Let's write out the function carefully.

```python
def count_atoms(formula):
    stack = [{}]
    i = 0
    n = len(formula)
    while i < n:
        char = formula[i]
        if char == '(':
            stack.append({})
            i += 1
        elif char == ')':
            i += 1
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            multiplier = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            top = stack.pop()
            for elem, count in top.items():
                stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count * multiplier
        elif char.isupper():
            start = i
            i += 1
            while i < n and formula[i].islower():
                i += 1
            elem = formula[start:i]
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            count = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count
        else:
            # Should not happen for valid formulas
            i += 1
            
    return dict(sorted(stack[0].items()))
```

The problem statement says `formula` is valid.

Let's write out the full function.

```python
def count_atoms(formula):
    stack = [{}]
    i = 0
    n = len(formula)
    while i < n:
        char = formula[i]
        if char == '(':
            stack.append({})
            i += 1
        elif char == ')':
            i += 1
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            multiplier = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            top = stack.pop()
            for elem, count in top.items():
                stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count * multiplier
        elif char.isupper():
            start = i
            i += 1
            while i < n and formula[i].islower():
                i += 1
            elem = formula[start:i]
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            count = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count
        else:
            i += 1
    return dict(sorted(stack[0].items()))
```

```python
def count_atoms(formula):
    stack = [{}]
    i = 0
    n = len(formula)
    while i < n:
        char = formula[i]
        if char == '(':
            stack.append({})
            i += 1
        elif char == ')':
            i += 1
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            multiplier = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            top = stack.pop()
            for elem, count in top.items():
                stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count * multiplier
        elif char.isupper():
            start = i
            i += 1
            while i < n and formula[i].islower():
                i += 1
            elem = formula[start:i]
            start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            count = int(formula[start:i]) if start < i else 1
            stack[-1][elem] = stack[-1].get(elem, 0) + count
        else:
            i += 1
    return dict(sorted(stack[0].items()))
```

task-0071-robot-footprints · Robot footprints

Prompt 
A cleaning robot starts at floor position (0,0) and executes a list of moves, each one of 'N','S','E','W' (one unit each). Write `robotCleaner(moves)` returning the number of DISTINCT grid cells the robot visits, counting the starting cell as visited once.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function robotCleaner(moves) {
  const visited = new Set();
  let x = 0, y = 0;
  visited.add('0,0');
  for (const m of moves) {
    if (m === 'N') y++;
    else if (m === 'S') y--;
    else if (m === 'E') x++;
    else if (m === 'W') x--;
    visited.add(`${x},${y}`);
  }
  return visited.size;
}
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0141-median-of-two-sorted-arrays · Median of two sorted arrays

Prompt 
Write `findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2)` returning the median of the two sorted arrays combined, as a number. If even length return the average of the two middle values. O(log(min(m,n))).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2) {
  if (nums1.length > nums2.length) {
    [nums1, nums2] = [nums2, nums1];
  }
  
  let m = nums1.length;
  let n = nums2.length;
  let low = 0;
  let high = m;
  
  while (low <= high) {
    let i = Math.floor((low + high) / 2);
    let j = Math.floor((m + n + 1) / 2) - i;
    
    let nums1_left = (i === 0) ? -Infinity : nums1[i - 1];
    let nums1_right = (i === m) ? Infinity : nums1[i];
    let nums2_left = (j === 0) ? -Infinity : nums2[j - 1];
    let nums2_right = (j === n) ? Infinity : nums2[j];
    
    if (nums1_left <= nums2_right && nums2_left <= nums1_right) {
      if ((m + n) % 2 === 1) {
        return Math.max(nums1_left, nums2_left);
      } else {
        return (Math.max(nums1_left, nums2_left) + Math.min(nums1_right, nums2_right)) / 2;
      }
    } else if (nums1_left > nums2_right) {
      high = i - 1;
    } else {
      low = i + 1;
    }
  }
  
  return 0;
}
