task-0187-counting-bits · Counting bits

Prompt 
Write `countBits(n)` returning an array ans of length n+1 where ans[i] is the number of 1-bits in the binary representation of i, for all 0..n. O(n) time.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `countBits`.
Model answer 
export function countBits(n) {
  const ans = new Array(n + 1);
  ans[0] = 0;
  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
    ans[i] = ans[i & (i - 1)] + 1;
  }
  return ans;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0148-split-array-largest-sum · Split array largest sum

Prompt 
Write `splitArray(nums, k)` returning the minimum possible largest sum among `k` contiguous subarrays when partitioning `nums`. Use binary search on the answer with a feasibility check, O(n log(sum)).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function splitArray(nums, k) {
    let left = 0;
    let right = 0;
    for (const num of nums) {
        left = Math.max(left, num);
        right += num;
    }

    while (left < right) {
        const mid = left + Math.floor((right - left) / 2);
        let count = 1;
        let currentSum = 0;
        for (const num of nums) {
            if (currentSum + num <= mid) {
                currentSum += num;
            } else {
                count++;
                currentSum = num;
            }
        }
        if (count <= k) {
            right = mid;
        } else {
            left = mid + 1;
        }
    }
    return left;
}
Reasoning Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0050-handshake-count-single · Handshake count single

Prompt 
In a room of 5 people, every person shakes hands with every other person exactly once. How many handshakes occur in total?
Model answer 
The user wants to find the total number of handshakes in a room of 5 people where everyone shakes hands with everyone else exactly once.

1.  **Identify the problem type:** This is a combinatorics problem. Specifically, it's about choosing 2 people out of 5 to form a handshake.
2.  **Formula:** The number of ways to choose $k$ items from a set of $n$ distinct items is given by the binomial coefficient $\binom{n}{k}$.
    *   Here, $n = 5$ (people).
    *   Here, $k = 2$ (handshake involves 2 people).
3.  **Calculation:**
    *   $\binom{5}{2} = \frac{5!}{2!(5-2)!} = \frac{5!}{2!3!} = \frac{5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1}{(2 \times 1)(3 \times 2 \times 1)} = \frac{5 \times 4}{2 \times 1} = \frac{20}{2} = 10$.
4.  **Alternative method (Summation):**
    *   Person 1 shakes hands with 4 others.
    *   Person 2 shakes hands with 3 others (excluding Person 1).
    *   Person 3 shakes hands with 2 others (excluding Persons 1 and 2).
    *   Person 4 shakes hands with 1 other (excluding Persons 1, 2, and 3).
    *   Person 5 has already shaken hands with everyone.
    *   Total = $4 + 3 + 2 + 1 = 10$.
5.  **Alternative method (Graph Theory):**
    *   This is a complete graph $K_5$. The number of edges is $\frac{n(n-1)}{2}$.
    *   $\frac{5(4)}{2} = \frac{20}{2} = 10$.

task-0279-plus-one-carry · Plus one (carry)

Prompt 
Given an array of digits representing a non-negative integer (no leading zeros), return the array of digits for that integer plus one. Example: [1,2,3] -> [1,2,4], [9,9] -> [1,0,0].

```js
// BUGGY
export function plusOne(digits) {
  digits[digits.length - 1] += 1;
  return digits;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `plusOne`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function plusOne(digits) {
  for (let i = digits.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    if (digits[i] < 9) {
      digits[i]++;
      return digits;
    }
    digits[i] = 0;
  }
  return [1, ...digits];
}

task-0243-largest-rectangle-in-histogram · Largest rectangle in histogram

Prompt 
Write `largestRectangleArea(heights)` where heights[i] is the height of a bar of width 1. Return the area of the largest rectangle that can be formed within the histogram. O(n) with a stack.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `largestRectangleArea`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function largestRectangleArea(heights) {
  const stack = [];
  let maxArea = 0;
  const n = heights.length;
  for (let i = 0; i <= n; i++) {
    const h = i === n ? 0 : heights[i];
    while (stack.length > 0 && h < heights[stack[stack.length - 1]]) {
      const height = heights[stack.pop()];
      const width = stack.length === 0 ? i : i - stack[stack.length - 1] - 1;
      maxArea = Math.max(maxArea, height * width);
    }
    stack.push(i);
  }
  return maxArea;
}
Reasoning Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0161-k-closest-points-to-origin · K closest points to origin

Prompt 
Write `kClosest(points, k)` returning the `k` points closest to the origin (0,0). Sort by squared Euclidean distance; ties broken by x then y ascending so output is deterministic.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function kClosest(points, k) {
  const sorted = [...points].sort((a, b) => {
    const d1 = a[0] * a[0] + a[1] * a[1];
    const d2 = b[0] * b[0] + b[1] * b[1];
    if (d1 !== d2) return d1 - d2;
    if (a[0] !== b[0]) return a[0] - b[0];
    return a[1] - b[1];
  });
  return sorted.slice(0, k);
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0212-halves-are-alike · Halves are alike

Prompt 
Write `halvesAreAlike(s)`. The string has even length. Return true if the first half and second half contain the same number of vowels (a, e, i, o, u, plus their uppercase forms).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `halvesAreAlike`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function halvesAreAlike(s) {
  const vowels = new Set("aeiouAEIOU");
  const mid = s.length / 2;
  let count1 = 0, count2 = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < mid; i++) {
    if (vowels.has(s[i])) count1++;
    if (vowels.has(s[mid + i])) count2++;
  }
  return count1 === count2;
}

task-0194-increasing-triplet-subsequence · Increasing triplet subsequence

Prompt 
Write `increasingTriplet(nums)` returning true if there exist i < j < k where nums[i] < nums[j] < nums[k]. O(n) time, O(1) space.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `increasingTriplet`.
Model answer 
export function increasingTriplet(nums) {
  let first = Infinity;
  let second = Infinity;
  for (const num of nums) {
    if (num <= first) {
      first = num;
    } else if (num <= second) {
      second = num;
    } else {
      return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}

task-0269-valid-parentheses-matching · Valid parentheses (matching)

Prompt 
Return true if the string has balanced, correctly nested brackets of types () [] {}. Example: "([{}])" is valid, "([)]" is not.

```js
// BUGGY
export function isValidParen(s) {
  const st = [];
  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch === "(" || ch === "[" || ch === "{") {
      st.push(ch);
    } else {
      if (!st.length) return false;
      st.pop();
    }
  }
  return st.length === 0;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `isValidParen`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function isValidParen(s) {
  const st = [];
  const pairs = { ')': '(', ']': '[', '}': '{' };
  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch === '(' || ch === '[' || ch === '{') {
      st.push(ch);
    } else {
      if (!st.length || st.pop() !== pairs[ch]) return false;
    }
  }
  return st.length === 0;
}

100%

task-0237-first-missing-positive · First missing positive

Prompt 
Write `firstMissingPositive(nums)` returning the smallest positive integer (>= 1) that is not present in the array. O(n) time, O(1) extra space.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `firstMissingPositive`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function firstMissingPositive(nums) {
    const n = nums.length;
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        while (nums[i] > 0 && nums[i] <= n && nums[nums[i] - 1] !== nums[i]) {
            const correctIdx = nums[i] - 1;
            const temp = nums[correctIdx];
            nums[correctIdx] = nums[i];
            nums[i] = temp;
        }
    }
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (nums[i] !== i + 1) {
            return i + 1;
        }
    }
    return n + 1;
}

100%

task-0204-sum-of-unique-elements · Sum of unique elements

Prompt 
Write `sumOfUnique(nums)` returning the sum of the elements that appear exactly once in `nums`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `sumOfUnique`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
function sumOfUnique(nums) {
  const counts = new Map();
  for (const num of nums) {
    counts.set(num, (counts.get(num) || 0) + 1);
  }
  let sum = 0;
  for (const [num, count] of counts) {
    if (count === 1) sum += num;
  }
  return sum;
}
export { sumOfUnique };

task-0234-trapping-rain-water · Trapping rain water

Prompt 
Write `trap(height)` where height[i] is the height of a bar. Compute how much water it can trap after rain. Example: `trap([0,1,0,2,1,0,1,3,2,1,2,1])` returns 6.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `trap`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function trap(height) {
    if (!height || height.length < 3) return 0;
    let left = 0;
    let right = height.length - 1;
    let left_max = 0;
    let right_max = 0;
    let water = 0;
    while (left < right) {
        if (height[left] < height[right]) {
            if (height[left] >= left_max) left_max = height[left];
            else water += left_max - height[left];
            left++;
        } else {
            if (height[right] >= right_max) right_max = height[right];
            else water += right_max - height[right];
            right--;
        }
    }
    return water;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0191-merge-strings-alternately · Merge strings alternately

Prompt 
Write `mergeAlternately(word1, word2)` that merges the strings by adding letters in alternating order starting with word1. If a string is longer, append the remaining letters.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `mergeAlternately`.
Model answer 
function mergeAlternately(word1, word2) {
  const res = [];
  const len = Math.max(word1.length, word2.length);
  for (let i = 0; i < len; i++) {
    if (i < word1.length) res.push(word1[i]);
    if (i < word2.length) res.push(word2[i]);
  }
  return res.join('');
}
export { mergeAlternately };

task-0122-squares-of-sorted-array · Squares of sorted array

Prompt 
Write `sortedSquares(nums)` where `nums` is sorted ascending (may contain negatives). Return an array of the squares of each number, sorted ascending. Use two pointers for O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function sortedSquares(nums) {
    const n = nums.length;
    const result = new Array(n);
    let left = 0;
    let right = n - 1;
    let pos = n - 1;

    while (left <= right) {
        const leftSq = nums[left] * nums[left];
        const rightSq = nums[right] * nums[right];
        if (leftSq > rightSq) {
            result[pos] = leftSq;
            left++;
        } else {
            result[pos] = rightSq;
            right--;
        }
        pos--;
    }

    return result;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0163-count-subsets-summing-to-target · Count subsets summing to target

Prompt 
Write `subset_sum_count(nums, target)` in Python returning the number of subsets (distinct index selections, non-empty or empty is fine) whose sum equals `target`. Values can be non-negative; use DP.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def subset_sum_count(nums, target):
    if target < 0:
        return 0
    dp = [0] * (target + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for num in nums:
        for j in range(target, num - 1, -1):
            dp[j] += dp[j - num]
    return dp[target]
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0125-longest-consecutive-sequence · Longest consecutive sequence

Prompt 
Write `longestConsecutive(nums)` returning the length of the longest consecutive elements sequence (values differing by 1) in the array, treating the array as an unordered set. O(n) time.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function longestConsecutive(nums) {
  const numSet = new Set(nums);
  let longest = 0;
  for (const num of numSet) {
    if (!numSet.has(num - 1)) {
      let currentNum = num;
      let currentStreak = 1;
      while (numSet.has(currentNum + 1)) {
        currentNum += 1;
        currentStreak += 1;
      }
      longest = Math.max(longest, currentStreak);
    }
  }
  return longest;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0201-squares-of-a-sorted-array · Squares of a sorted array

Prompt 
Write `sortedSquares(nums)` given a sorted (ascending) array of integers, returning a new array of the squares of each number sorted in ascending order.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `sortedSquares`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function sortedSquares(nums) {
  const result = new Array(nums.length);
  let left = 0;
  let right = nums.length - 1;
  let index = nums.length - 1;
  
  while (left <= right) {
    const leftSq = nums[left] ** 2;
    const rightSq = nums[right] ** 2;
    if (leftSq > rightSq) {
      result[index--] = leftSq;
      left++;
    } else {
      result[index--] = rightSq;
      right--;
    }
  }
  
  return result;

task-0219-validate-ip-address · Validate IP address

Prompt 
Write `validIPAddress(queryIP)` returning "IPv4" if it is a valid IPv4 address, "IPv6" if a valid IPv6 address, else "Neither". IPv4: four decimal groups 0-255 with no leading zeros. IPv6: eight groups of 1-4 hex digits separated by colons.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `validIPAddress`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function validIPAddress(queryIP) {
  if (queryIP.includes('.')) {
    const parts = queryIP.split('.');
    if (parts.length !== 4) return "Neither";
    for (const part of parts) {
      if (part.length === 0 || part.length > 3) return "Neither";
      if (!/^\d+$/.test(part)) return "Neither";
      if (part.length > 1 && part[0] === '0') return "Neither";
      if (parseInt(part, 10) > 255) return "Neither";
    }
    return "IPv4";
  } else if (queryIP.includes(':')) {
    const parts = queryIP.split(':');
    if (parts.length !== 8) return "Neither";
    for (const part of parts) {
      if (part.length === 0 || part.length > 4) return "Neither";
      if (!/^[0-9a-fA-F]+$/.test(part)) return "Neither";
    }
    return "IPv6";
  }
  return "Neither";
}
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0235-sliding-window-maximum · Sliding window maximum

Prompt 
Write `maxSlidingWindow(nums, k)` returning an array containing the max of each contiguous subarray of length k, using a monotonic deque for O(n) time.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `maxSlidingWindow`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function maxSlidingWindow(nums, k) {
  if (!nums.length || k <= 0) return [];
  const result = [];
  const deque = [];
  let head = 0;
  
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (head < deque.length && deque[head] <= i - k) {
      head++;
    }
    
    while (head < deque.length && nums[deque[deque.length - 1]] <= nums[i]) {
      deque.pop();
    }
    
    deque.push(i);
    
    if (i >= k - 1) {
      result.push(nums[deque[head]]);
    }
  }
  
  return result;
}
Mathematics Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0218-decode-string · Decode string

Prompt 
Write `decodeString(s)`. The encoding rule is k[encoded_string] where the text inside the brackets is repeated exactly k times. The input is always valid. Example: `decodeString("3[a]2[bc]")` returns `"aaabcbc"`. Output can be long.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `decodeString`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function decodeString(s) {
    const stack = [];
    let currentNum = 0;
    let currentStr = "";
    
    for (const char of s) {
        if (char >= '0' && char <= '9') {
            currentNum = currentNum * 10 + Number(char);
        } else if (char === '[') {
            stack.push(currentNum);
            stack.push(currentStr);
            currentNum = 0;
            currentStr = "";
        } else if (char === ']') {
            const prevStr = stack.pop();
            const repeatNum = stack.pop();
            currentStr = prevStr + currentStr.repeat(repeatNum);
        } else {
            currentStr += char;
        }
    }
    
    return currentStr;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0138-min-subarray-length-sum-target · Min subarray length sum >= target

Prompt 
Write `minSubArrayLen(target, nums)` returning the minimal length of a contiguous subarray whose sum is >= `target`, or 0 if none. Sliding window, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function minSubArrayLen(target, nums) {
    let left = 0;
    let currentSum = 0;
    let minLength = Infinity;
    for (let right = 0; right < nums.length; right++) {
        currentSum += nums[right];
        while (currentSum >= target) {
            minLength = Math.min(minLength, right - left + 1);
            currentSum -= nums[left];
            left++;
        }
    }
    return minLength === Infinity ? 0 : minLength;
}

task-0156-min-arrows-to-burst-balloons · Min arrows to burst balloons

Prompt 
Write `findMinArrowShots(points)` where `points[i] = [xstart, xend]` are balloon horizontal extents (inclusive). An arrow shot at x bursts all balloons containing x. Return the minimum number of arrows to burst all balloons.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function findMinArrowShots(points) {
    if (points.length === 0) return 0;
    points.sort((a, b) => a[1] - b[1]);
    let arrows = 1;
    let currentEnd = points[0][1];
    for (let i = 1; i < points.length; i++) {
        if (points[i][0] > currentEnd) {
            arrows++;
            currentEnd = points[i][1];
        }
    }
    return arrows;
}

task-0091-campus-network-cabling · Campus network cabling

Prompt 
A network planner must cable together all campus buildings with minimal total wiring. Each building is a point [x, y]; the cost to connect two buildings is the Manhattan distance |x1-x2| + |y1-y2|. Write `min_span_cost(points)` returning the minimum total cost to connect all buildings so every pair is connected (a minimum spanning tree). Use Prim's algorithm, O(n^2).

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def f(...)`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def min_span_cost(points):
    n = len(points)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0
    
    min_dist = [float('inf')] * n
    min_dist[0] = 0
    visited = [False] * n
    total_cost = 0
    
    for _ in range(n):
        u = -1
        best_dist = float('inf')
        for i in range(n):
            if not visited[i] and min_dist[i] < best_dist:
                best_dist = min_dist[i]
                u = i
                
        visited[u] = True
        total_cost += best_dist
        
        for v in range(n):
            if not visited[v]:
                dist = abs(points[u][0] - points[v][0]) + abs(points[u][1] - points[v][1])
                if dist < min_dist[v]:
                    min_dist[v] = dist
                    
    return total_cost
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0082-token-bucket-rate-limiter · Token bucket rate limiter

Prompt 
An API gateway uses a token bucket to rate-limit requests. The bucket starts full with `capacity` tokens and refills continuously at `refillPerSec` tokens per second, capped at `capacity`. `events` is a time-ordered list of [timeSeconds, cost]; process events in order: first refill using the elapsed time since the previous event (the first event's elapsed time starts from 0), then if at least `cost` tokens are available deduct them (request allowed, record true) else the request is denied (record false). Fractional tokens are allowed. Write `tokenBucket(events, capacity, refillPerSec)` returning an array of booleans, one per event.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function tokenBucket(events, capacity, refillPerSec) {
  let currentTokens = capacity;
  let lastTime = 0;
  const results = [];
  for (const [time, cost] of events) {
    currentTokens = Math.min(capacity, currentTokens + (time - lastTime) * refillPerSec);
    if (currentTokens >= cost) {
      results.push(true);
      currentTokens -= cost;
    } else {
      results.push(false);
    }
    lastTime = time;
  }
  return results;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 9/9 cases
100%

task-0183-is-subsequence · Is subsequence

Prompt

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `isSubsequence`.
Model answer 
export function isSubsequence(s, t) {
  let i = 0;
  for (let j = 0; j < t.length && i < s.length; j++) {
    if (t[j] === s[i]) i++;
  }
  return i === s.length;
}

task-0205-ransom-note · Ransom note

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `canConstruct`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function canConstruct(ransomNote, magazine) {
  const counts = {};
  for (const char of magazine) {
    counts[char] = (counts[char] || 0) + 1;
  }
  for (const char of ransomNote) {
    if (!counts[char]) return false;
    counts[char]--;
  }
  return true;
}

task-0126-task-scheduler · Task scheduler

Prompt 
Write `least_interval(tasks, n)` in Python. `tasks` is a list of letters where each letter is a task type. Return the least number of unit time slots needed so the same task type is separated by at least `n` units of idle time.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def least_interval(tasks, n):
    if not tasks:
        return 0
    freq = {}
    for t in tasks:
        freq[t] = freq.get(t, 0) + 1
    max_freq = max(freq.values())
    max_count = sum(1 for v in freq.values() if v == max_freq)
    return max(len(tasks), (max_freq - 1) * (n + 1) + max_count)

task-0213-left-and-right-sum-differences · Left and right sum differences

Prompt 
Write `leftRigthDifference(nums)` returning an array where element i is the absolute difference between the sum of elements to the left of i and the sum of elements to the right of i (use 0 for empty sides).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `leftRigthDifference`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function leftRigthDifference(nums) {
  const totalSum = nums.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
  const result = [];
  let leftSum = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const rightSum = totalSum - leftSum - nums[i];
    result.push(Math.abs(leftSum - rightSum));
    leftSum += nums[i];
  }
  return result;
}

task-0214-longest-palindromic-substring · Longest palindromic substring

Prompt 
Write `longestPalindrome(s)` returning the longest palindromic substring of `s`. If there are multiple of the same length, return the one starting earliest. Example: `longestPalindrome("babad")` returns `"bab"`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `longestPalindrome`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function longestPalindrome(s) {
  if (!s) return "";
  let start = 0;
  let maxLen = 0;
  
  const expand = (l, r) => {
    while (l >= 0 && r < s.length && s[l] === s[r]) {
      l--;
      r++;
    }
    return r - l - 1;
  };
  
  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) {
    const len1 = expand(i, i);
    const len2 = expand(i, i + 1);
    const len = Math.max(len1, len2);
    
    if (len > maxLen) {
      maxLen = len;
      start = i - Math.floor((len - 1) / 2);
    }
  }
  
  return s.substring(start, start + maxLen);
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 15/15 cases
100%

task-0178-integer-to-roman · Integer to Roman

Prompt 
Write `intToRoman(num)` converting an integer 1..3999 to a Roman numeral. Use standard symbols and subtractive forms (IV, IX, XL, XC, CD, CM).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `intToRoman`.
Model answer 
export function intToRoman(num) {
  const values = [1000, 900, 500, 400, 100, 90, 50, 40, 10, 9, 5, 4, 1];
  const symbols = ["M", "CM", "D", "CD", "C", "XC", "L", "XL", "X", "IX", "V", "IV", "I"];
  let result = "";
  for (let i = 0; i < values.length; i++) {
    while (num >= values[i]) {
      result += symbols[i];
      num -= values[i];
    }
  }
  return result;
}

task-0106-trace-a-slow-request-across-services · Trace a slow request across services

Prompt 
A user request that normally takes 50ms now takes 5 seconds. The request chains through three backend microservices. Describe your ordered cross-service investigation: reproduce the slow request, enable or inspect distributed tracing, examine logs and metrics for each hop, identify the service where latency spikes, find the root cause (e.g. lock contention, a slow query, or degraded dependency), and describe how to confirm the fix.
Model answer 
**1. Reproduce the Slow Request**
- Capture the exact request: endpoint, HTTP method, payload, headers, auth token, and client IP/region.
- Reproduce in a staging environment with production-like data volume, config, and network topology.
- Run the request repeatedly to confirm consistent 5s latency vs. the 50ms baseline. Record request ID/correlation ID for downstream tracking.

**2. Enable & Inspect Distributed Tracing**
- Verify tracing is active (OpenTelemetry, Jaeger, Zipkin, etc.). If not, inject `traceparent`/`tracestate` headers or enable SDK auto-instrumentation.
- Map the trace across the three services: `Client → Svc1 → Svc2 → Svc3`.
- Inspect span durations, network vs. processing time, and tags for errors, retries, or circuit-breaker states. Note which span consumes the bulk of the 5s.

**3. Examine Logs & Metrics for Each Hop**
- Correlate logs to the trace ID per service. Look for warnings, retries, timeout errors, or connection pool exhaustion.
- Check per-service metrics: CPU, memory, GC pauses, thread pool saturation, queue depth, and I/O wait.
- Check downstream dependencies: DB query latency, cache hit/miss ratio, external API response times, message queue lag, and DNS/SSL handshake delays.
- Identify anomalies: sudden spikes, saturation thresholds, retry storms, or degraded health checks.

**4. Identify Latency Spike & Root Cause**
- Compare span durations to isolate the exact service/hop causing the delay.
- Drill into that service using targeted diagnostics:
  - **Slow query:** Check DB slow-query logs, `EXPLAIN` plans, missing indexes, or full table scans.
  - **Lock contention:** Analyze thread dumps, DB deadlock logs, distributed lock timeouts, or mutex/wait queue depth.
  - **Degraded dependency:** Verify downstream service health, timeout/retry misconfigurations, connection pool exhaustion, or certificate/SSL renegotiation delays.
  - **Resource exhaustion:** CPU throttling, memory pressure/GC pauses, disk I/O saturation, or network bandwidth limits.
- Use APM flame graphs, eBPF network/IO traces, or profiling tools if logs/metrics are inconclusive.

**5. Confirm the Fix**
- Apply the targeted fix (e.g., query optimization, index addition, lock timeout adjustment, pool scaling, dependency patch, or config correction).
- Re-run the exact reproduction request in staging; verify latency returns to ~50ms.
- Run controlled load tests to ensure no regression under normal and peak traffic.
- Monitor APM, logs, and infrastructure metrics for 24–48 hours to confirm stability.
- Document the root cause, update runbooks, and add alerts for the specific metric that flagged the issue (e.g., slow query threshold, lock wait time, or connection pool saturation).

task-0223-jump-game · Jump game

Prompt 
Write `canJump(nums)`. You start at index 0, and from index i you can jump up to nums[i] steps ahead. Return true if you can reach the last index.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `canJump`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function canJump(nums) {
    let maxReach = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
        if (i > maxReach) return false;
        maxReach = Math.max(maxReach, i + nums[i]);
        if (maxReach >= nums.length - 1) return true;
    }
    return true;
}

task-0073-directory-resolver · Directory resolver

Prompt 
A shell agent navigates a filesystem. Starting at the root directory `/`, it applies a list of navigation `steps` in order. '..' goes up one level (if already at root, it stays at root), '.' does nothing, and any other string is a directory name that is entered. Write `resolver(steps)` returning the final absolute path as a string (e.g. '/a/b'). At the root return '/'. Steps are lowercase directory names and special tokens '..' and '.'.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function resolver(steps) {
  const parts = [];
  for (const step of steps) {
    if (step === '..') {
      if (parts.length > 0) parts.pop();
    } else if (step !== '.') {
      parts.push(step);
    }
  }
  return '/' + parts.join('/');
}

task-0198-longest-substring-without-repeating-char · Longest substring without repeating characters

Prompt 
Write `length_of_longest_substring(s)` in Python returning the length of the longest substring without repeating characters.

Output ONLY the function `length_of_longest_substring`.
Model answer 
def length_of_longest_substring(s):
    last_seen = {}
    start = 0
    max_len = 0
    for i, c in enumerate(s):
        if c in last_seen and last_seen[c] >= start:
            start = last_seen[c] + 1
        last_seen[c] = i
        if i - start + 1 > max_len:
            max_len = i - start + 1
    return max_len

task-0090-tic-tac-toe-game-solver · Tic-tac-toe game solver

Prompt 
A game agent computes the game-theoretic value of a tic-tac-toe position using minimax. `board` is a string of 9 characters ('0'..'8' read left-to-right, top-to-bottom), each 'X', 'O', or '.' (empty). It is X's turn to move, and no one has won yet. Write `ticTacToeResult(board)` returning 1 if X can force a win with perfect play, 0 if the best X can do is a draw, and -1 if O can force a win. X maximizes, O minimizes. Rows/cols/diagonals of three identical marks win.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
The user wants a function `ticTacToeResult(board)` that computes the game-theoretic value of a tic-tac-toe position using minimax.
The board is a string of 9 characters ('0'..'8' read left-to-right, top-to-bottom), each 'X', 'O', or '.' (empty).

Since it's tic-tac-toe, the state space is small. We can use memoization or just compute directly.

Let's write a helper to check if a player has won.
Actually, the problem says "no one has won yet" for the initial call, but during recursion, we need to check if the current player has won or if the previous move caused a win. Wait, minimax usually checks after a move.
Since it's X's turn to move initially, we will generate moves for X, then check if X wins. If not, recurse for O.
Actually, standard minimax:
function minimax(board, isMaximizing):
  if checkWin(board, 'X'): return 1
  if checkWin(board, 'O'): return -1
  if board is full: return 0
  if isMaximizing:
    best = -infinity
    for each empty cell:
      board[cell] = 'X'
      val = minimax(board, false)
      best = max(best, val)
      board[cell] = '.'
    return best
  else:
    best = infinity
    for each empty cell:
      board[cell] = 'O'
      val = minimax(board, true)
      best = min(best, val)
      board[cell] = '.'
    return best

Wait, the problem says "It is X's turn to move, and no one has won yet."
So we can just call minimax(board, true) or handle it directly.
But we need to be careful: if X makes a move and wins, that's a win for X. If O makes a move and wins, that's a win for O.
The base cases should check wins after the move is made.
Actually, it's easier to check wins at the beginning of the function. But if we check at the beginning, we need to handle the case where the board is passed with a win already. Since the initial call says no one has won, we can just check wins after placing a mark, or at the start.
Let's check wins at the start. If X wins, return 1. If O wins, return -1.
But wait, if we check at the start, the initial call will return 0 or recurse.
Actually, standard approach:
function solve(board, isX):
  if checkWin(board, 'X') return 1
  if checkWin(board, 'O') return -1
  if no empty cells return 0
  if isX:
    res = -1
    for i in 0..8:
      if board[i] == '.':
        board[i] = 'X'
        res = max(res, solve(board, false))
        board[i] = '.'
        if res == 1: break // alpha-beta pruning optional, but not needed for 9 cells
    return res
  else:
    res = 1
    for i in 0..8:
      if board[i] == '.':
        board[i] = 'O'
        res = min(res, solve(board, true))
        board[i] = '.'
        if res == -1: break
    return res

Wait, checking wins at the start is fine. But we must ensure that when we recurse, we pass the updated board.

  Returns min of results.
This works.

Winning lines:
[0,1,2], [3,4,5], [6,7,8],
[0,3,6], [1,4,7], [2,5,8],
[0,4,8], [2,4,6]

Let's implement checkWin(board, player):
  for each line in lines:

```javascript
export function ticTacToeResult(board) {
    const lines = [
        [0,1,2], [3,4,5], [6,7,8],
        [0,3,6], [1,4,7], [2,5,8],
        [0,4,8], [2,4,6]
    ];
    
    function checkWin(b, p) {
        for (const l of lines) {
            if (b[l[0]] === p && b[l[1]] === p && b[l[2]] === p) return true;
        }
        return false;
    }
    
    function minimax(b, isX) {
        if (checkWin(b, 'X')) return 1;
        if (checkWin(b, 'O')) return -1;
        
        let empty = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
            if (b[i] === '.') empty.push(i);
        }
        if (empty.length === 0) return 0;
        
        if (isX) {
            let best = -1;
            for (const i of empty) {
                b[i] = 'X';
                best = Math.max(best, minimax(b, false));
                b[i] = '.';
                if (best === 1) break;
            }
            return best;
        } else {
            let best = 1;
            for (const i of empty) {
                b[i] = 'O';
                best = Math.min(best, minimax(b, true));
                b[i] = '.';
                if (best === -1) break;
            }
            return best;
        }
    }
    
    // Convert string to array for mutability
    let boardArr = board.split('');
    return minimax(boardArr, true);
}
```

```javascript
export function ticTacToeResult(board) {
    const lines = [
        [0,1,2], [3,4,5], [6,7,8],
        [0,3,6], [1,4,7], [2,5,8],
        [0,4,8], [2,4,6]
    ];
    
    function checkWin(b, p) {
        for (const l of lines) {
            if (b[l[0]] === p && b[l[1]] === p && b[l[2]] === p) return true;
        }
        return false;
    }
    
    function minimax(b, isX) {
        if (checkWin(b, 'X')) return 1;
        if (checkWin(b, 'O')) return -1;
        
        let empty = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
            if (b[i] === '.') empty.push(i);
        }
        if (empty.length === 0) return 0;
        
        if (isX) {
            let best = -1;
            for (const i of empty) {
                b[i] = 'X';
                best = Math.max(best, minimax(b, false));
                b[i] = '.';
                if (best === 1) break;
            }
            return best;
        } else {
            let best = 1;
            for (const i of empty) {
                b[i] = 'O';
                best = Math.min(best, minimax(b, true));
                b[i] = '.';
                if (best === -1) break;
            }
            return best;
        }
    }
    
    return minimax(board.split(''), true);
}
```

task-0174-climbing-stairs · Climbing stairs

Prompt 
You are climbing a staircase. It takes `n` steps to reach the top. Each time you can climb 1 or 2 steps. Write `climbStairs(n)` returning the number of distinct ways to reach the top. `n` is between 1 and 45.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `climbStairs`.
Model answer 
export function climbStairs(n) {
  if (n <= 2) return n;
  let a = 1, b = 2;
  for (let i = 3; i <= n; i++) {
    let c = a + b;
    a = b;
    b = c;
  }
  return b;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 10/10 cases
100%

task-0165-count-derangements · Count derangements

Prompt 
Write `derangements(n)` returning the number of permutations of n items in which no element appears in its original position (derangements), computed exactly by DP: D(0)=1, D(1)=0, D(n)=(n-1)*(D(n-1)+D(n-2)). n in [0, 20].

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function derangements(n) {
  if (n === 0) return 1;
  if (n === 1) return 0;
  let prev2 = 1;
  let prev1 = 0;
  for (let i = 2; i <= n; i++) {
    let curr = (i - 1) * (prev1 + prev2);
    prev2 = prev1;
    prev1 = curr;
  }
  return prev1;
}
Knowledge Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0111-target-sum-pair-count · Target sum pair count

Prompt 
Write a function `pairCount(nums, target)` that returns the number of (unordered) index pairs (i, j) with i < j such that `nums[i] + nums[j] === target`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function pairCount(nums, target) {
  const freq = new Map();
  let count = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    const complement = target - num;
    count += freq.get(complement) || 0;
    freq.set(num, (freq.get(num) || 0) + 1);
  }
  return count;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0254-factorial · Factorial

Prompt

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `factorial`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function factorial(n) {
  let r = 1;
  for (let i = 2; i <= n; i++) {
    r *= i;
  }
  return r;
}

task-0188-missing-number · Missing number

Prompt 
Write `missing_number(nums)` in Python. Given an array containing n distinct numbers taken from 0..n, return the one number in 0..n that is missing from the array. O(n) time, O(1) space.

Output ONLY the function `missing_number`.
Model answer 
def missing_number(nums):
    n = len(nums)
    return n * (n + 1) // 2 - sum(nums)

task-0295-longest-palindromic-substring · Longest palindromic substring

Prompt 
Return the longest palindromic substring of s. If multiple have the same max length, return the earliest one. Example: "babad" -> "bab", "cbbd" -> "bb".

```python
# BUGGY
def longest_palindrome(s):
    def expand(l, r):
        while l >= 0 and r < len(s) and s[l] == s[r]:
            l -= 1
            r += 1
        return s[l + 1:r]
    best = ""
    for i in range(len(s)):
        cand = expand(i, i)
        if len(cand) > len(best):
            best = cand
    return best
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def longest_palindrome(s):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def longest_palindrome(s):
    def expand(l, r):
        while l >= 0 and r < len(s) and s[l] == s[r]:
            l -= 1
            r += 1
        return s[l + 1:r]
    
    best = ""
    for i in range(len(s)):
        cand = expand(i, i)
        if len(cand) > len(best):
            best = cand
        cand = expand(i, i + 1)
        if len(cand) > len(best):
            best = cand
    return best

task-0189-kids-with-greatest-candies · Kids with greatest candies

Prompt 
Write `kidsWithCandies(candies, extra)` returning a boolean array where result[i] is true if, after giving the kid extra candies, they have the greatest number of candies among all kids (ties allowed).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `kidsWithCandies`.
Model answer 
export function kidsWithCandies(candies, extra) {
  const max = Math.max(...candies);
  return candies.map(c => c + extra >= max);
}

task-0151-min-initial-health-in-grid · Min initial health in grid

Prompt 
Write `calculate_min_hp(dungeon)` in Python. `dungeon` is an m x n grid; negative cells reduce health, positive cells restore it, 0 is neutral. You start at top-left and move only right/down; health can never drop to 0 or below. Return the minimum initial health needed to reach bottom-right. DP from bottom-right.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def calculate_min_hp(dungeon):
    m, n = len(dungeon), len(dungeon[0])
    dp = [[0] * n for _ in range(m)]
    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):
        for j in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                dp[i][j] = max(1, 1 - dungeon[i][j])
            elif i == m - 1:
                dp[i][j] = max(1, dp[i][j+1] - dungeon[i][j])
            elif j == n - 1:
                dp[i][j] = max(1, dp[i+1][j] - dungeon[i][j])
            else:
                dp[i][j] = max(1, min(dp[i+1][j], dp[i][j+1]) - dungeon[i][j])
    return dp[0][0]
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0220-simplify-path · Simplify path

Prompt 
Write `simplifyPath(path)` canonicalizing an absolute Unix-style file path: collapse multiple slashes, resolve "." and ".." (which goes up one level), and produce a path starting with a single "/". Example: `simplifyPath("/a//b/../../c/")` returns `"/c"`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `simplifyPath`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function simplifyPath(path) {
  const parts = path.split('/');
  const stack = [];
  for (const part of parts) {
    if (part === '' || part === '.') {
      continue;
    } else if (part === '..') {
      if (stack.length > 0) {
        stack.pop();
      }
    } else {
      stack.push(part);
    }
  }
  return '/' + stack.join('/');
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0119-count-negatives-in-grid · Count negatives in grid

Prompt 
Write `countNegatives(grid)` returning the number of negative numbers in an `m x n` grid where each row is sorted in decreasing order (and each column is sorted in decreasing order).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function countNegatives(grid) {
    if (!grid || grid.length === 0) return 0;
    const m = grid.length;
    const n = grid[0].length;
    let row = 0;
    let col = n - 1;
    let count = 0;
    while (row < m && col >= 0) {
        if (grid[row][col] < 0) {
            count += m - row;
            col--;
        } else {
            row++;
        }
    }
    return count;
}
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0298-coin-change-min-coins · Coin change (min coins)

Prompt 
Return the fewest number of coins needed to make up 'amount', or -1 if impossible. Example: coins=[1,2,5], amount=11 -> 3 (5+5+1).

```python
# BUGGY
def coin_change(coins, amount):
    INF = float("inf")
    dp = [INF] * (amount + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    for a in range(amount + 1):
        for c in coins:
            if a >= c:
                dp[a] = min(dp[a], dp[a - c])
    return dp[amount] if dp[amount] != INF else -1
```

task-0228-repeated-dna-sequences · Repeated DNA sequences

Prompt 
Write `findRepeatedDnaSequences(s)` returning all 10-letter-long substrings that occur more than once in the DNA string. Return them sorted lexicographically. Example: `findRepeatedDnaSequences("AAAAACCCCCAAAAACCCCCCAAAAAGGGTTT")` returns `["AAAAACCCCC","CCCCCAAAAA"]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findRepeatedDnaSequences`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function findRepeatedDnaSequences(s) {
  if (s.length < 10) return [];
  const seen = new Set();
  const repeated = new Set();
  for (let i = 0; i <= s.length - 10; i++) {
    const sub = s.slice(i, i + 10);
    if (seen.has(sub)) {
      repeated.add(sub);
    } else {
      seen.add(sub);
    }
  }
  return [...repeated].sort();
}

task-0277-longest-common-prefix · Longest common prefix

Prompt

```js
// BUGGY
export function longestCommonPrefix(strs) {
  if (!strs.length) return "";
  let pref = strs[0];
  for (let i = 1; i < strs.length; i++) {
    while (pref.length && !strs[i].startsWith(pref)) {
      pref = pref.slice(1);
    }
  }
  return pref;
```

export function longestCommonPrefix(strs) {
  if (!strs.length) return "";

  for (let i = 1; i < strs.length; i++) {

  }

task-0170-min-candies-distribution · Min candies distribution

Prompt 
Write `candy(ratings)` in Python. Children sit in a row with `ratings[i]`. Each child must get at least 1 candy, and a child with a higher rating than a neighbor must get more candies than that neighbor. Return the minimum total candies. Two-pass left/right arrays.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def candy(ratings):
    n = len(ratings)
    if n == 0:
        return 0
    left = [1] * n
    right = [1] * n
    for i in range(1, n):
        if ratings[i] > ratings[i-1]:
            left[i] = left[i-1] + 1
    for i in range(n-2, -1, -1):
        if ratings[i] > ratings[i+1]:
            right[i] = right[i+1] + 1
    return sum(max(l, r) for l, r in zip(left, right))
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0192-greatest-common-divisor-of-strings · Greatest common divisor of strings

Prompt 
Write `gcdOfStrings(str1, str2)` returning the largest string `x` that divides both str1 and str2 (str1 = x repeated k times, str2 = x repeated m times). Return "" if none.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `gcdOfStrings`.
Model answer 
function gcd(a, b) {
  while (b) {
    [a, b] = [b, a % b];
  }
  return a;
}

export function gcdOfStrings(str1, str2) {
  if (str1 + str2 !== str2 + str1) return "";
  return str1.slice(0, gcd(str1.length, str2.length));
}

task-0182-move-zeroes · Move zeroes

Prompt 
Write `moveZeroes(nums)` that moves all zeroes to the end while maintaining the relative order of non-zero elements, in place, and returns nums.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `moveZeroes`.
Model answer 
function moveZeroes(nums) {
    let insertPos = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
        if (nums[i] !== 0) {
            nums[insertPos++] = nums[i];
        }
    }
    while (insertPos < nums.length) {
        nums[insertPos++] = 0;
    }
    return nums;
}

export { moveZeroes };
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 11/11 cases

task-0118-is-perfect-square · Is perfect square

Prompt 
Write `is_perfect_square(n)` in Python returning True if `n` is a perfect square (non-negative integer square), else False. Do not rely on floating point root; use integer arithmetic.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def is_perfect_square(n):
    if n < 0:
        return False
    if n == 0:
        return True
    low, high = 1, n
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        sq = mid * mid
        if sq == n:
            return True
        elif sq < n:
            low = mid + 1
        else:
            high = mid - 1
    return False
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0143-word-break · Word break

Prompt 
Write `wordBreak(s, wordDict)` returning true if `s` can be segmented into words from `wordDict` (words can be reused). DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function wordBreak(s, wordDict) {
    const dict = new Set(wordDict);
    const n = s.length;
    const dp = new Array(n + 1).fill(false);
    dp[0] = true;
    for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
        for (let j = 0; j < i; j++) {
            if (dp[j] && dict.has(s.substring(j, i))) {
                dp[i] = true;
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    return dp[n];
}

task-0284-climbing-stairs · Climbing stairs

You can climb 1 or 2 steps at a time. Return the number of distinct ways to reach the top of n steps (n >= 1).

```python
# BUGGY
def climb_stairs(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return 1
    prev, cur = 1, 1
    for _ in range(2, n):
        prev, cur = cur, prev + cur
    return cur
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def climb_stairs(n):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def climb_stairs(n):
    a, b = 1, 1
    for _ in range(n):
        a, b = b, a + b
    return a

task-0166-multiply-large-numbers · Multiply large numbers

Prompt 
Write `multiply(num1, num2)` taking two non-negative integer strings and returning their product as a string without converting the whole thing to a native integer. Use grade-school digit multiplication.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
The user wants a function `multiply(num1, num2)` that multiplies two non-negative integer strings using grade-school digit multiplication.
The output should be an ES module exporting the function.
No native integer conversion for the whole number.
Output only the function definition.

Grade-school multiplication:
- Initialize a result array of size `len1 + len2` with zeros.
- Iterate through digits of `num1` from right to left.
- Iterate through digits of `num2` from right to left.
- Multiply digits, add to the appropriate position in the result array, handle carry.
- Convert result array to string, removing leading zeros.

Let's write it out carefully.

```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';
  
  const len1 = num1.length;
  const len2 = num2.length;
  const res = new Array(len1 + len2).fill(0);
  
  for (let i = len1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = len2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';
}
```

Wait, `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);` might cause `res[p1]` to exceed 9 temporarily, but that's fine because the next iteration or the final pass will handle it? Actually, in grade school multiplication, we process from right to left, so `res[p1]` might accumulate carries from multiple multiplications. But since we process `j` from right to left, `res[p1]` gets updated multiple times. It's safer to just let it accumulate and then do a final pass to normalize carries, or handle it correctly.

Actually, the standard approach:
```javascript

  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';

      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';

```
Wait, `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);` might make `res[p1]` greater than 9. But since we process from right to left, when we move to the next `j` or `i`, we might add to `res[p1]` again. Actually, `res[p1]` is the position to the left. It will eventually be processed when `i` or `j` changes? No, `res[p1]` is just an accumulator. At the end, we need to normalize carries. Or we can just do a final pass to handle carries.

Let's trace: `num1 = "123", num2 = "456"`
len1=3, len2=3, res size 6.
i=2 ('3'), j=2 ('6'): mul=18, p1=4, p2=5. sum=18+0=18. res[5]=8, res[4]+=1. res=[0,0,0,0,1,8]
i=2, j=1 ('5'): mul=15, p1=3, p2=4. sum=15+1=16. res[4]=6, res[3]+=1. res=[0,0,0,1,6,8]

i=1, j=0 ('4'): mul=8, p1=1, p2=2. sum=8+2=10. res[2]=0, res[1]+=1. res=[0,1,0,4,8,8]

Result: "56088". Correct.
Wait, `res[1]` became 5, but `res[0]` is 0. So it works.
But what if `res[p1]` accumulates more than 9? In the above trace, `res[p1]` only got incremented by 1 each time because `sum % 10` handles the carry to `p1`. But `res[p1]` itself could be > 9 if multiple carries land there before being processed. However, since we process from right to left, `res[p1]` is to the left. It will never be processed as `p2` in the current `i` loop because `p2` is always `i+j+1`, and `p1` is `i+j`. For a fixed `i`, as `j` decreases, `p1` and `p2` decrease. So `res[p1]` for current `j` becomes `res[p2]` for next `j`? No.
Actually, `res[p1]` is just an accumulator. It might exceed 9. But at the end, we just convert to string. If `res[p1]` is 12, it will be converted to "12", which is wrong. We need to normalize carries at the end, or handle them properly.

Let's fix the carry handling properly:
```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';
  
  const len1 = num1.length;
  const len2 = num2.length;
  const res = new Array(len1 + len2).fill(0);
  
  for (let i = len1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = len2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  // Normalize carries
  for (let k = res.length - 1; k > 0; k--) {
    if (res[k] >= 10) {
      res[k - 1] += Math.floor(res[k] / 10);
      res[k] %= 10;
    }
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';
}
```
Wait, the normalization loop is correct. But actually, in the standard algorithm, `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10)` is fine because `res[p1]` will eventually be processed when `i` or `j` changes? No, `res[p1]` is just an index. It's safer to normalize at the end.

It won't because `res[0]` is the most significant digit, and max product of two n-digit numbers has at most 2n digits. `res[0]` will be at most 9. Actually, `res[0]` could be >9 if we don't normalize? Let's check `num1="999", num2="999"`.

This is safe and correct.

Let's stick with the normalization pass to be safe. Or even simpler: just convert each digit to string and join, but handle carries properly. Actually, the standard LeetCode solution for Multiply Strings does exactly this without a separate normalization pass because the carry propagation happens naturally or is handled. But adding a normalization pass is harmless and correct.

Wait, `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);` might make `res[p1]` large. The normalization pass handles it.

Let's refine:

Actually, in the loop, `res[p1]` accumulates carries. At the end, `res` might have values > 9. But when we convert to string, `digit` will be e.g., 12, and `result += 12` gives "12", which is wrong. So we definitely need to normalize carries at the end, or handle them during the loop.

Let's add a simple normalization pass:
```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';
  
  const len1 = num1.length;
  const len2 = num2.length;
  const res = new Array(len1 + len2).fill(0);
  
  for (let i = len1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = len2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  // Normalize carries
  for (let k = res.length - 1; k > 0; k--) {
    res[k - 1] += Math.floor(res[k] / 10);
    res[k] %= 10;
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';
}
```
Wait, the normalization pass should go from right to left, which it does. But `res[k]` could be >= 10. `res[k-1] += Math.floor(res[k]/10)` handles it. This is correct.

Actually, we can simplify the normalization by just doing:
```javascript
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    result += digit;
  }
  // But this doesn't handle carries > 9 properly if we just concatenate.
```
So normalization is needed.

```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';
  
  const len1 = num1.length;
  const len2 = num2.length;
  const res = new Array(len1 + len2).fill(0);
  
  for (let i = len1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = len2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';
}
```

Let's add the normalization loop. It's safe.

Wait, another approach: instead of `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10)`, we can just keep it and do a final pass to normalize. Or we can just use `res[p1] = Math.floor(sum / 10)`? No, because `res[p1]` accumulates.

Actually, the standard way is:
```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === '0' || num2 === '0') return '0';
  
  const len1 = num1.length;
  const len2 = num2.length;
  const res = new Array(len1 + len2).fill(0);
  
  for (let i = len1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = len2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1[i] - '0') * (num2[j] - '0');
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + res[p2];
      
      res[p2] = sum % 10;
      res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
    }
  }
  
  let result = '';
  for (const digit of res) {
    if (!(result === '' && digit === 0)) {
      result += digit;
    }
  }
  
  return result || '0';
}
```
I'll test `num1="99", num2="99"` again.
After loops: `res=[9, 8, 0, 1]`. Correct.
What about `num1="999", num2="999"`?
i=2,j=2: mul=81, p1=4,p2=5. sum=81. res[5]=1, res[4]+=8. res=[0,0,0,0,8,1]
i=2,j=1: mul=81, p1=3,p2=4. sum=81+8=89. res[4]=9, res[3]+=8. res=[0,0,0,8,9,1]
i=2,j=0: mul=81, p1=2,p2=3. sum=81+8=89. res[3]=9, res[2]+=8. res=[0,0,8,9,9,1]
i=1,j=2: mul=81, p1=3,p2=4. sum=81+9=90. res[4]=0, res[3]+=9. res=[0,0,8,17,0,1]
i=1,j=1: mul=81, p1=2,p2=3. sum=81+17=98. res[3]=8, res[2]+=9. res=[0,0,17,8,0,1]
i=1,j=0: mul=81, p1=1,p2=2. sum=81+17=98. res[2]=8, res[1]+=9. res=[0,9,8,8,0,1]
i=0,j=2: mul=81, p1=2,p2=3. sum=81+8=89. res[3]=9, res[2]+=8. res=[0,9,16,9,0,1]
i=0,j=1: mul=81, p1=1,p2=2. sum=81+16=97. res[2]=7, res[1]+=9. res=[0,18,7,9,0,1]
i=0,j=0: mul=81, p1=0,p2=1. sum=81+18=99. res[1]=9, res[0]+=9. res=[9,9,7,9,0,1]
Result: "997901". Correct. `res[1]` was 18 temporarily, but then `sum=81+18=99`, `res[1]=9`, `res[0]+=9`. So it worked out without explicit normalization because the carry from `res[1]` was added to `res[0]` in the last step. But `res[0]` became 9, which is fine.

So the algorithm naturally handles carries because the most significant position `res[0]` is only updated in the last iteration of `i=0, j=0`. And `res[0]` will be at most 9 because the maximum product of two n-digit numbers has at most 2n digits. So `res[0]` won't exceed 9. Thus, explicit normalization is not strictly necessary, but it doesn't hurt. I'll keep it simple without the extra pass, as it's standard.

Wait, what if `num1="0", num2="0"`? Handled.
What if `num1="1", num2="1"`? Handled.
What if `num1="123", num2="0"`? Returns "0". Correct.

One minor thing: `res[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);` might make `res[p1]` > 9, but as shown, it gets resolved. However, to be absolutely safe and clean, I'll add a final pass to normalize, or just use `res[p1] = Math.floor(sum / 10)`? No, `res[p1]` accumulates.

Actually, the standard LeetCode solution does exactly this without normalization pass. I'll stick to that.

task-0117-frequency-of-maximum · Frequency of maximum

Prompt 
Write `maxCount(nums)` returning how many times the maximum value appears in the array. Return 0 for an empty array.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxCount(nums) {
  if (nums.length === 0) return 0;
  const max = Math.max(...nums);
  return nums.filter(n => n === max).length;
}

task-0127-jump-game · Jump game

Prompt 
Write `canJump(nums)` where `nums[i]` is the maximum jump length from position i. Return true if you can reach the last index, else false. Greedy, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function canJump(nums) {
  let maxReach = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (i > maxReach) return false;
    maxReach = Math.max(maxReach, i + nums[i]);
    if (maxReach >= nums.length - 1) return true;
  }
  return false;
}

task-0085-election-scrutineer · Election scrutineer

Prompt 
An election-counting agent must find the candidate who received strictly more than half of the votes. Given `nums` where each value is a vote for candidate `nums[i]`, a majority candidate is guaranteed to exist. Write `majorityElement(nums)` returning the majority value. Use Boyer-Moore voting for O(n) time and O(1) space.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function majorityElement(nums) {
  let candidate = null;
  let count = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    if (count === 0) {
      candidate = num;
      count = 1;
    } else if (num === candidate) {
      count++;
    } else {
      count--;
    }
  }
  return candidate;
}

task-0268-two-sum-fix-reuse · Two sum (fix reuse)

Prompt 
Return the indices (ascending) of the two numbers that add up to target. Exactly one solution exists and you may not use the same element twice.

```js
// BUGGY
export function twoSum(nums, target) {
  const m = new Map();
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    m.set(nums[i], i);
    const need = target - nums[i];
    if (m.has(need)) return [m.get(need), i].sort((a, b) => a - b);
  }
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `twoSum`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function twoSum(nums, target) {
  const m = new Map();
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const need = target - nums[i];
    if (m.has(need)) return [m.get(need), i].sort((a, b) => a - b);
    m.set(nums[i], i);
  }
}

task-0034-integer-partitions · Integer partitions

Prompt 
Write `partitions(n)` in Python returning the number of integer partitions of `n` — the count of ways to write `n` as a sum of positive integers where order does not matter. n is in [1, 60]. Use the coin-change style DP over the coin values 1..n.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def partitions(n):
    dp = [0] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for coin in range(1, n + 1):
        for j in range(coin, n + 1):
            dp[j] += dp[j - coin]
    return dp[n]

task-0168-number-of-coin-combinations · Number of coin combinations

Prompt 
Write `coinChange2(amount, coins)` returning the number of distinct combinations of coins (order does NOT matter) that sum to `amount`, using unlimited coins. 1D DP: `dp[s] += dp[s - c]` iterating coins outer loop.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function coinChange2(amount, coins) {
  const dp = new Array(amount + 1).fill(0);
  dp[0] = 1;
  for (const c of coins) {
    for (let s = c; s <= amount; s++) {
      dp[s] += dp[s - c];
    }
  }
  return dp[amount];
}

task-0302-median-of-two-sorted-arrays · Median of two sorted arrays

Prompt 
Return the median of two sorted arrays a and b combined. Example: a=[1,3], b=[2] -> 2; a=[1,2], b=[3,4] -> 2.5.

```js
// BUGGY
export function findMedian(a, b) {
  const merged = a.concat(b).sort((x, y) => x - y);
  const mid = Math.floor(merged.length / 2);
  return merged[mid];
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `findMedian`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function findMedian(a, b) {
  if (a.length > b.length) {
    [a, b] = [b, a];
  }
  const m = a.length;
  const n = b.length;
  let left = 0;
  let right = m;

  while (left <= right) {
    const i = Math.floor((left + right) / 2);
    const j = Math.floor((m + n + 1) / 2) - i;

    const aLeft = i === 0 ? -Infinity : a[i - 1];
    const aRight = i === m ? Infinity : a[i];
    const bLeft = j === 0 ? -Infinity : b[j - 1];
    const bRight = j === n ? Infinity : b[j];

    if (aLeft <= bRight && bLeft <= aRight) {
      if ((m + n) % 2 === 0) {
        return (Math.max(aLeft, bLeft) + Math.min(aRight, bRight)) / 2;
      } else {
        return Math.max(aLeft, bLeft);
      }
    } else if (aLeft > bRight) {
      right = i - 1;
    } else {
      left = i + 1;
    }
  }
  return 0;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

```python
    return ans
```

    ans = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        ans[i] = ans[i // 2] + (i % 2)
    return ans

task-0211-maximum-69-number · Maximum 69 number

Prompt 
Write `maximum69Number(num)`. The number contains only digits 6 and 9. You may change at most one digit (6 to 9, or 9 to 6). Return the maximum possible number. Changing the leftmost 6 to a 9 is optimal.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `maximum69Number`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function maximum69Number(num) {
  return Number(num.toString().replace('6', '9'));
}

task-0247-merge-k-sorted-lists · Merge k sorted lists

Prompt 
Write `mergeKLists(lists)` where lists is an array of sorted (ascending) integer arrays. Merge them all into one sorted array and return it.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `mergeKLists`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function mergeKLists(lists) {
  const heap = [];
  const res = [];
  
  for (let i = 0; i < lists.length; i++) {
    if (lists[i] && lists[i].length) {
      heap.push({ v: lists[i][0], i, j: 0 });
    }
  }
  
  const siftDown = (idx) => {
    const n = heap.length;
    while (true) {
      let s = idx;
      const l = 2 * idx + 1, r = 2 * idx + 2;
      if (l < n && heap[l].v < heap[s].v) s = l;
      if (r < n && heap[r].v < heap[s].v) s = r;
      if (s !== idx) {
        [heap[idx], heap[s]] = [heap[s], heap[idx]];
        idx = s;
      } else break;
    }
  };
  
  const siftUp = (idx) => {
    while (idx > 0) {
      const p = (idx - 1) >> 1;
      if (heap[idx].v < heap[p].v) {
        [heap[idx], heap[p]] = [heap[p], heap[idx]];
        idx = p;
      } else break;
    }
  };
  
  for (let i = Math.floor(heap.length / 2) - 1; i >= 0; i--) siftDown(i);
  
  while (heap.length) {
    const min = heap[0];
    res.push(min.v);
    heap[0] = heap[heap.length - 1];
    heap.pop();
    if (heap.length) siftDown(0);
    
    const ni = min.i, nj = min.j + 1;
    if (nj < lists[ni].length) {
      heap.push({ v: lists[ni][nj], i: ni, j: nj });
      siftUp(heap.length - 1);
    }
  }
  
  return res;
}
Mathematics Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0030-integral-of-x-2-x-on-0-2 · Integral of x^2 + x on [0,2]

Prompt 
Evaluate the definite integral ∫₀² (x² + x) dx. Which value is correct?
Model answer 
The user wants to evaluate the definite integral $\int_0^2 (x^2 + x) \, dx$.

1.  **Find the antiderivative:**
    The integrand is $f(x) = x^2 + x$.
    The antiderivative $F(x)$ is found by integrating term by term.
    $\int x^2 \, dx = \frac{x^3}{3}$
    $\int x \, dx = \frac{x^2}{2}$
    So, $F(x) = \frac{x^3}{3} + \frac{x^2}{2}$.

2.  **Apply the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus:**
    $\int_0^2 (x^2 + x) \, dx = F(2) - F(0)$.

3.  **Evaluate $F(2)$:**
    $F(2) = \frac{2^3}{3} + \frac{2^2}{2} = \frac{8}{3} + \frac{4}{2} = \frac{8}{3} + 2$.
    Convert 2 to a fraction with denominator 3: $2 = \frac{6}{3}$.
    $F(2) = \frac{8}{3} + \frac{6}{3} = \frac{14}{3}$.

4.  **Evaluate $F(0)$:**
    $F(0) = \frac{0^3}{3} + \frac{0^2}{2} = 0 + 0 = 0$.

5.  **Calculate the result:**
    $\int_0^2 (x^2 + x) \, dx = \frac{14}{3} - 0 = \frac{14}{3}$.

6.  **Convert to decimal (optional but good for checking):**
    $14 / 3 = 4.666...$

7.  **Format the answer:**
    The user asks for the single best option, preferably just the letter. However, no options are provided in the prompt.
    Wait, the prompt says "Which value is correct?" but doesn't list options A, B, C, D.

task-0020-functional-composition-evaluation-order · Functional composition evaluation order

Prompt 
```js
const double = x => x * 2;
const inc = x => x + 1;
const compose = (...fns) => x => fns.reduceRight((acc, f) => f(acc), x);
console.log(compose(double, inc)(5));
```
What number does this print?
Model answer 
12

**Step-by-step evaluation:**
1. `compose(double, inc)` returns a new function that takes an argument `x`.
2. Inside the returned function, `fns` is the array `[double, inc]`.
3. `reduceRight` iterates over the array from **right to left**.
4. First iteration: applies `inc` to `x` (5) → `inc(5)` = `6`.
5. Second iteration: applies `double` to the accumulator (6) → `double(6)` = `12`.
6. The final result `12` is printed.
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 9/9 cases
100%

task-0080-deadline-resource-scheduler · Deadline resource scheduler

Prompt 
A resource-scheduling agent must schedule the maximum number of tasks. Each task is [duration, deadline] (deadline exclusive; tasks are sequential and take integer time). The agent must pick a subset of tasks and an ordering so that each completes by its deadline. Write `schedule(tasks)` returning the maximum number of tasks that can all be scheduled. Algorithm: sort by deadline, process in order, and when the running total exceeds a deadline drop the longest-duration task so far.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function schedule(tasks) {
  tasks.sort((a, b) => a[1] - b[1]);
  const heap = [];
  let total = 0;
  
  const push = (val) => {
    heap.push(val);
    let i = heap.length - 1;
    while (i > 0) {
      const parent = (i - 1) >> 1;
      if (heap[parent] >= heap[i]) break;
      [heap[parent], heap[i]] = [heap[i], heap[parent]];
      i = parent;
    }
  };
  
  const pop = () => {
    const max = heap[0];
    const end = heap.pop();
    if (heap.length > 0) {
      heap[0] = end;
      let i = 0;
      const n = heap.length;
      while (true) {
        let largest = i;
        const l = 2 * i + 1, r = 2 * i + 2;
        if (l < n && heap[l] > heap[largest]) largest = l;
        if (r < n && heap[r] > heap[largest]) largest = r;
        if (largest === i) break;
        [heap[i], heap[largest]] = [heap[largest], heap[i]];
        i = largest;
      }
    }
    return max;
  };
  
  for (const [dur, deadline] of tasks) {
    push(dur);
    total += dur;
    if (total > deadline) {
      total -= pop();
    }
  }
  
  return heap.length;
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0077-keyword-ranking-agent · Keyword ranking agent

Prompt 
A content-analysis agent ranks keywords. Given `text` and an integer `k`, write `topWords(text, k)` returning the top `k` most frequent words (sequences of letters only, case-insensitive). Tally frequencies; sort by descending frequency, and break ties by ascending word. Return the top `k` words. If fewer than `k` distinct words exist, return all of them.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function topWords(text, k) {
  const words = text.toLowerCase().match(/[a-z]+/g) || [];
  const freq = new Map();
  for (const w of words) {
    freq.set(w, (freq.get(w) || 0) + 1);
  }
  const sorted = Array.from(freq.entries()).sort((a, b) => {
    if (b[1] !== a[1]) return b[1] - a[1];
    return a[0] < b[0] ? -1 : 1;
  });
  return sorted.slice(0, k).map(e => e[0]);
}
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0056-probability-conditional · Probability conditional

Prompt 
A family has two children. You are told that at least one of them is a boy. What is the probability that both children are boys? (Assume boy and girl equally likely and independent.)
Model answer 
The user wants me to solve a probability puzzle.

**Problem Statement:**
A family has two children.
Condition: At least one of them is a boy.
Question: What is the probability that both children are boys?
Assumptions: Boy and girl are equally likely ($P(B) = P(G) = 0.5$) and independent.

**Sample Space:**
Let the two children be Child 1 and Child 2.
Possible outcomes (ordered):
1. (Boy, Boy) - BB
2. (Boy, Girl) - BG
3. (Girl, Boy) - GB
4. (Girl, Girl) - GG

Total number of outcomes = 4.
Each outcome has probability $1/4$.

**Condition:**
"At least one of them is a boy."
This eliminates the outcome (Girl, Girl) - GG.
Remaining outcomes:
1. (Boy, Boy) - BB
2. (Boy, Girl) - BG
3. (Girl, Boy) - GB

Number of favorable outcomes for the condition = 3.

**Target Event:**
"Both children are boys."
This corresponds to the outcome (Boy, Boy) - BB.
Number of favorable outcomes for the target event = 1.

**Calculation:**
$P(\text{Both Boys} | \text{At least one Boy}) = \frac{P(\text{Both Boys} \cap \text{At least one Boy})}{P(\text{At least one Boy})}$

$P(\text{Both Boys} \cap \text{At least one Boy}) = P(\text{Both Boys}) = 1/4$.
$P(\text{At least one Boy}) = 1 - P(\text{No Boys}) = 1 - P(GG) = 1 - 1/4 = 3/4$.

Alternatively, using the reduced sample space:
Total outcomes in reduced space = 3 (BB, BG, GB).
Target outcomes in reduced space = 1 (BB).

Probability = $1/3$.

**Verification:**
This is a classic probability puzzle.
If the information was "The older child is a boy", the answer would be $1/2$ (BB, BG).
If the information was "One of them is a boy named Florida" (rare name), the answer approaches $1/2$.
But with just "at least one is a boy", the standard answer is $1/3$.

**Conclusion:**
The probability is $1/3$.
